The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth will be basis for judgment when they rush to Lord.

**A Verse That Warns and Gives Hope**

This verse both warns and gives hope. Warns, because rushing from graves to Lord means accountability, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) but will be recompensed for what it did. Gives hope, because rushing to Lord can also be rushing to mercy, as believing man was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and Paradise residents will have peace from Merciful Lord (36:58). The rushing is to Lord who is Most Merciful (36:5, 36:58), so rushing to Him is not only fearful but also hopeful for those who followed Reminder (36:11). The sūrah says *“Yet they will all be brought before Us”* (36:32) — all, believer and disbeliever, will rush, but outcomes differ. The verse invites reader to consider how he will rush: as one who followed messengers who ask no reward (36:21) and worshiped Originator (36:22), or as one who turned away from signs (36:46) and refused to donate (36:47) and mocked threat (36:48). Rushing is inevitable; preparation determines whether rushing ends in Paradise busy enjoying (36:55) or Hell burning (36:64).

**The Link to Authority Over All Things**

Finally, rushing from graves to Lord shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83). Graves cannot hold when He commands; bodies that were decayed bones (36:78) rush when trumpet blown. The One who created heavens and earth can easily resurrect (36:81), and trumpet is means. The verse is short, but it holds complete eschatology: trumpet blown, graves opened, rushing to Lord. It answers the mocking question *“When will this threat come?”* (36:48) with description: it will come as trumpet and rushing, not at time you choose. It also answers doubt *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) with demonstration: He who blows trumpet and makes them rush. The reader is invited to hear trumpet now as reminder, to rush now from farthest end of heedlessness toward messengers, before forced rushing from graves to Lord.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:52

> They will cry, “Woe to us! Who has raised us up from our place of rest? This must be what the Most Compassionate warned us of; the messengers told the truth!”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Cry of Woe Upon Resurrection**

*“They will cry, Woe to us! Who has raised us up from our place of rest? This must be what the Most Compassionate warned us of; the messengers told the truth”* (36:52) records what those rushing from graves (36:51) will say. Yā waylanā, woe to us, is exclamation of regret and fear. Marqad, place of rest, sleep, indicates graves were rest, sleep, from which they are raised, baʿatha, same verb used for resurrection. The question who has raised us shows surprise, as if they did not expect raising despite being warned. The sūrah earlier said *“They ask, When will this threat come, if you are truthful?”* (36:48) mocking threat; now threat has come and they cry woe. The second part — *“This must be what the Most Compassionate warned us of; the messengers told the truth”* (36:52) — is acknowledgment that what they mocked is now realized. It can be read as continuation of their own speech, realizing truth, or as angels' answer to them: this is what Most Compassionate promised and messengers spoke truth. Both readings fit: disbelievers realize, or angels inform. Either way, truth of promise and messengers is affirmed at moment when acknowledgment no longer benefits.

**Why Place of Rest Is Mentioned**

Place of rest, marqad, suggests graves were restful compared to what follows, or that they were sleeping, unaware. The Qur'an says *“They will say, Woe to us, who has raised us from our place of sleep?”* indicating that death was like sleep, and resurrection is awakening. The sūrah earlier said *“There is also a sign for them in the night: We strip daylight, then behold, they are in darkness”* (36:37) — night is sleep, day is awakening, similar to death and resurrection. Place of rest also indicates that graves were not punishment yet, but waiting. The punishment comes after raising, when *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64). The cry woe to us shows that raising itself is terrifying because they know what follows. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54), so woe is not because they are wronged but because they wronged themselves and now face consequence.

**Most Compassionate Warned Us**

They say *“This must be what the Most Compassionate warned us of”* (36:52), using al-Raḥmān, Most Compassionate, same name town said revealed nothing (36:15) and believing man said could intend harm (36:23). Now they acknowledge that Most Compassionate did warn, and warning was truth. The use of Most Compassionate for warning shows that warning itself was mercy, as earlier *“so that you may warn a people whose forefathers were not warned”* (36:6) was mercy. They mocked warning as threat, now see it as what Most Compassionate warned of, acknowledging mercy they rejected. The sūrah says *“Peace will be their greeting from Merciful Lord”* (36:58) for Paradise residents, showing that Most Compassionate's mercy includes both warning before and peace after for those who believed. For those who disbelieved, Most Compassionate's warning becomes reality they face.

**Messengers Told the Truth**

*“The messengers told the truth”* (36:52) is acknowledgment that messengers who were mocked (36:30), called liars (36:15), threatened with stoning (36:18), and whose follower was killed and told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26), were truthful. The acknowledgment comes too late to benefit, but it is recorded as proof that message was clear. The sūrah says *“Our duty is only to deliver the message clearly”* (36:17), and messengers did, and now even deniers admit they told truth. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth includes this acknowledgment, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on record that includes both clear delivery and late acknowledgment. The verse thus shows that truth of messengers will be universally acknowledged on Day, even by those who denied in world.

**How This Verse Connects to Earlier Mocking**

This verse directly answers mocking question *“When will this threat come, if you are truthful?”* (36:48). The answer is now: threat has come as raising from place of rest, and it is what Most Compassionate warned of, and messengers told truth. The mocking question assumed threat would never come or that truthful would know timing; now threat has come without timing being known, and truthfulness is affirmed. The sūrah says *“They must be awaiting a single Blast, which will seize them while they are entrenched in worldly disputes”* (36:49) — blast seized, then trumpet raised, and now they cry woe. The sequence shows that mocking does not prevent threat; it only delays recognition until too late. The believing man who declared *“I believe in your Lord, so listen to me”* (36:25) and was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) did not need to cry woe; he wished his people knew forgiveness and honor (36:27). His early acknowledgment saved him from late woe.

**A Verse That Defines Late Recognition**

Finally, this verse defines late recognition: woe, surprise, acknowledgment that Most Compassionate warned and messengers told truth, but recognition comes after raising, when no bequest can be made (36:50) and no return to people (36:50). The Qur'an says *“On Day when some signs of your Lord come, belief will not benefit soul that did not believe before”* (6:158). Raising from place of rest is sign whose belief after seeing does not benefit. The verse invites reader to recognize now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), that Most Compassionate did warn and messengers told truth, so that he will not be among those crying woe on Day, but among those busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55), reclining in shade (36:56), having fruits and whatever they desire (36:57), greeted with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58). The choice is between early recognition leading to Paradise and late recognition leading to woe.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:53

> It will only take one Blast, then at once they will all be brought before Us

**Expanded Commentary**

**One Blast Brings All Before God**

*“It will only take one Blast, then at once they will all be brought before Us”* (36:53) describes second blast that raises from graves. After first blast seizing while disputing (36:49) and leaving no bequest or return (36:50), and trumpet blown and rushing from graves (36:51) and crying woe (36:52), this verse says one blast brings all before God. Ṣayḥah wāḥidah, single cry, blast, is same phrase used for town extinguished (36:29) and for blast seizing while disputing (36:49). One blast suffices for destruction and for gathering. Fa-idhā hum jamīʿun ladaynā muḥḍarūn, then at once they will all be brought before Us, emphasizes immediacy and universality. At once, fa-idhā, suddenly, all, jamīʿ, all together, before Us, ladaynā, in Our presence, brought, muḥḍarūn, made present. The sūrah earlier said *“Yet they will all be brought before Us”* (36:32), and this verse says how — by one blast, at once. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth will be basis for judgment when all brought.

**Why One Blast Suffices**

One blast suffices because divine command is sufficient. The Qur'an says *“All it takes, when He wills something to be, is simply to say to it: Be! And it is”* (36:82). Blast is like Be — command that brings effect. The town extinguished at once by one blast (36:29) showed that destruction needs one blast; this verse shows gathering also needs one blast. The same power that destroys can gather. The sūrah says *“We did not send soldiers from heavens against his people after his death, nor did We need to”* (36:28) — no army needed, one blast enough. The verse also shows ease of resurrection: if one blast can bring all before God, resurrection is not difficult. The sūrah later says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81). One blast bringing all is easy for Master Creator.

**At Once as Suddenness**

At once, fa-idhā, indicates suddenness, no gradual process. Those in graves are at once brought before God. The Qur'an says *“It is but a single cry, then behold, they will be in the open plain”* (79:13-14) about resurrection. Suddenness shows that resurrection is not slow evolution but instant command. The sūrah earlier said *“Then they will not be able to make a last will, nor can they return to their own people”* (36:50) about first blast; second blast also leaves no time to remain in graves. The immediacy is warning: prepare now, because when blast comes, at once you will be brought, with no time to add to record. The believing man who rushed from farthest end (36:20) and declared belief (36:25) was prepared, so his being brought was to Paradise (36:26); those who turned away from signs (36:46) and refused to donate (36:47) and mocked threat (36:48) will be brought to judgment that leads to *“Burn in it today”* (36:64).

**All Brought Before Us as Accountability**

All brought before Us, jamīʿun ladaynā muḥḍarūn, emphasizes accountability. All — believers, disbelievers, town that rejected, man who believed, ancestors carried in Ark, those carried in ships — all brought. Before Us indicates presence before God, not just gathering in place. The Qur'an says *“They will be presented before your Lord in rows”* (18:48). Presentation is humbling because it is before One in Whose Hands is authority over all things (36:83). The town that said *“you will be touched with painful punishment from us”* (36:18), claiming authority, will be brought before One whose punishment is truly painful. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) was brought before God in honor. Same gathering, different outcomes, based on what was sent forth and left behind (36:12) and recorded in perfect Record. The verse thus asserts both universality of gathering and individuality of outcome.

**How This Verse Connects to Previous Verses**

This verse connects to *“Yet they will all be brought before Us”* (36:32) earlier, which said all will be brought, and to *“The Trumpet will be blown, then behold, they will rush from graves to their Lord”* (36:51), which described rushing. This verse summarizes: one blast, at once, all brought before Us. It also connects to *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) next, which gives principle of judgment when all brought. The sequence is: trumpet blown, rushing from graves, crying woe, acknowledgment that Most Compassionate warned and messengers told truth, then one blast brings all before Us, then no soul wronged, rewarded only for what it did. The sequence is logical: gathering, then judgment. The sūrah's structure moves from signs of power to scenes of gathering to principles of judgment to descriptions of Paradise and Hell.

**A Verse That Defines Ease of Resurrection**

Finally, this verse defines ease of resurrection: one blast, at once, all brought. It is easy for God, hard for humans to imagine, but easy for Master Creator, All-Knowing (36:81). The verse invites reader to trust that resurrection will happen as easily as described, and to prepare for being brought before God by following Reminder (36:11), following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22), giving thanks for provision (36:35, 36:73), and seeking mercy while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44). The One who can bring all before Him with one blast can also forgive and honor with one word *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and greet with *“Peace from Merciful Lord”* (36:58). The choice is between being brought to punishment or to peace, and preparation now determines outcome when one blast brings all before Us.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:54

> On that Day no soul will be wronged in the least, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do

**Expanded Commentary**

**Justice on Day of Gathering**

*“On that Day no soul will be wronged in the least, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) gives principle of judgment when all are brought before God (36:32, 36:53). No soul wronged, lā tuẓlamu nafsun shayʾā, means no injustice, no extra punishment beyond deeds, no good deed ignored. The Qur'an says *“God does not wrong people in the least, but people wrong themselves”* (10:44) and *“Whoever does an atom's weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom's weight of evil will see it”* (99:7-8). Perfect Record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth and left behind ensures no wrong. The second clause — nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do — means recompense is based on deeds, not arbitrary. The sūrah earlier said *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead, and write what they send forth and leave behind”* (36:12), so reward based on what was written. The verse thus defines justice as accurate record and proportionate recompense.

**Why No Soul Wronged Is Emphasized**

Emphasis on no soul wronged is needed because gathering all at once by one blast (36:53) might suggest hasty, inaccurate judgment. The verse says no, judgment will be precise, no soul wronged in least. The Qur'an says *“We will set up scales of justice for Day of Resurrection, so no soul will be wronged in least”* (21:47). Scales ensure accuracy. The sūrah says *“Everything is listed in a perfect Record”* (36:12) — perfect record ensures no wrong. The town extinguished at once (36:29) might seem collective punishment that wrongs individuals, but perfect record shows each individual's deeds, and no soul wronged. The believing man killed and told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) was not wronged; his killing was recorded and honored with forgiveness and being among honoured (36:27). The verse assures that even when punishment seems collective — single blast seizing while disputing (36:49) — individual justice remains, because record is individual.

**Reward Except for What You Used to Do**

Reward except for what you used to do, illā mā kuntum taʿmalūn, means recompense corresponds to deeds. Good deeds lead to Paradise described as busy enjoying, shade, couches, fruits, peace (36:55-58); evil deeds lead to Hell described as *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64). The correspondence is not that deeds earn Paradise by themselves, but that deeds are basis for mercy to grant reward. The sūrah says *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen. So give them good news of forgiveness and honourable reward”* (36:11) — forgiveness and reward are given, not just earned, but they are given to those who follow and fear. The verse says you will not be rewarded except for what you used to do, indicating that reward is tied to deeds, so those who followed will be rewarded, those who turned away (36:46), refused to donate (36:47), mocked threat (36:48) will be recompensed accordingly.

**How This Verse Relates to Previous and Next**

This verse follows gathering all before God (36:53) and precedes description of Paradise residents busy enjoying (36:55) and Hell residents burning (36:63-64). It is principle that explains both descriptions: Paradise residents enjoy because they did good — followed Reminder, worshiped Originator, gave thanks — and Hell residents burn because they did evil — turned away, refused to donate, mocked. The verse is hinge between gathering and outcomes. The sūrah's structure is: signs (36:33-44), call to be mindful (36:45), turning away (36:46), refusal and mocking (36:47-48), blasts and gathering (36:49-53), principle of no wrong (36:54), then Paradise and Hell scenes (36:55-64), then power to seal mouths and make hands speak (36:65). Principle of no wrong ensures that scenes that follow are just, not arbitrary. The reader is invited to see Paradise and Hell as just recompense for what was sent forth and left behind.

**A Verse That Defines Accountability**

This verse defines accountability: no soul wronged, rewarded only for what it did. Accountability requires record, which perfect Record provides (36:12), and witnesses, which hands and feet provide (36:65). The sūrah says *“On this Day We will seal their mouths, their hands will speak to Us, and their feet will testify”* (36:65), showing that even if mouth denies, limbs testify, ensuring no wrong. The town that threatened *“you will be touched with painful punishment from us”* (36:18) will find that its own hands that threatened will speak against it. The believing man whose feet rushed from farthest end (36:20) will have feet testify for him. Accountability is thus embodied. The verse also says you will not be rewarded except for what you used to do, implying that intercession from powerless idols (36:23, 36:74-75) will not help, because reward is based on deeds, not on idols' intercession. Only intercession by permission of Most Compassionate benefits (20:109), and permission is for those whose deeds pleased Him.

**The Link to Mercy and Authority**

Finally, no soul wronged and rewarded only for what it did shows both justice and mercy, and that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83). Justice ensures no wrong; mercy ensures forgiveness and honourable reward for those who believed, as believing man received forgiveness and being among honoured (36:27). The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of justice: gathering all at once by one blast (36:53) does not lead to hasty injustice; perfect Record ensures accuracy; limbs testify; no soul wronged; reward based on deeds. The reader is invited to ensure that what he used to do is what will lead to being among those busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55), reclining in shade (36:56), having fruits and whatever they desire (36:57), greeted with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58), rather than among those told to step away (36:59), reminded of command not to follow Satan (36:60-61), told this is Hell you were warned of (36:63), burn in it today (36:64). Choice is now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:55

> Indeed, on that Day the residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves

**Expanded Commentary**

**Paradise Residents Busy Enjoying**

*“Indeed, on that Day the residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) begins description of Paradise after principle of no wrong (36:54). Aṣḥāb al-jannah, companions, residents of Garden, are described as fī shughlin fākihūn, busy, occupied, enjoying, rejoicing. Shughl indicates being busy with delight, absorbed in enjoyment, not idle. Fākihūn means enjoying, rejoicing, with connotation of delight and humor. The sūrah says they will be busy enjoying themselves, implying that Paradise is not boredom but active delight. The description follows gathering all before God (36:53) and principle of no wrong (36:54), showing outcome for those who followed Reminder (36:11), followed those who ask no reward (36:21), worshiped Originator (36:22), gave thanks (36:35, 36:73). Their being busy enjoying contrasts with those in this world busy disputing (36:49) and unable to make bequest (36:50). One busyness is in worldly disputes that end with no bequest; other busyness is in Paradise enjoyment that never ends.

**Why Busy Enjoying Is Mentioned**

Busy enjoying is mentioned to show that Paradise is not static but dynamic, with delights that occupy. Some exegetes say inhabitants are busy with delights of Paradise and spiritual realities, absorbed in vision of God, which is highest delight. Others say different levels of Paradise have different occupations, all delightful. The sūrah later says they and spouses reclining on couches in shade (36:56), having fruits and whatever they desire (36:57), greeted with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58). Busy enjoying encompasses all those. The phrase on that Day, al-yawm, indicates Day of Resurrection, Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53), Day when no soul wronged (36:54). On that Day, two groups emerge: busy enjoying and burning in Hell (36:63-64). The contrast is intentional. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and made among honoured (36:27) is among those busy enjoying, wishing his people knew (36:26-27). His busyness in Paradise includes wishing guidance for others, showing that enjoyment does not erase compassion.

**How This Verse Relates to Previous Signs**

This verse relates to earlier signs: dead earth revived with grain for eating (36:33), gardens of palms and grapevines and springs (36:34) for eating fruit (36:35), pairs created (36:36), night and day, sun and moon (36:37-40), Ark and similar things to ride (36:41-42), cattle created and subjected (36:71-72). All those provisions are foretastes of Paradise provision. Grain, gardens, springs, fruits, shade, spouses, couches — all have worldly counterparts that are signs. Paradise residents busy enjoying themselves enjoy perfected versions of those worldly provisions, without toil, without end. The sūrah says *“Therein they have fruit and whatsoever they call for”* (36:57) — fruits like worldly fruits but superior. The busy enjoying is thus continuation and perfection of worldly enjoyment that was by mercy for little while (36:44). Those who gave thanks for little while enjoyment (36:35, 36:73) will have everlasting enjoyment; those who turned away (36:46) and refused to donate (36:47) will not.

**The Contrast With Hell**

This verse begins Paradise description that contrasts with Hell description that follows: *“Then the disbelievers will be told, Step away this Day, O wicked ones! Did I not command you not to follow Satan? This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:59-64). The contrast is between busy enjoying and burning, between being with spouses in shade reclining (36:56) and being sealed mouths with hands speaking (36:65). The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54), so both outcomes are just. The busy enjoying is reward for what they used to do (36:54) — following Reminder, fearing unseen, donating, worshiping Originator. The verse invites reader to choose which busyness he wants on that Day: busy disputing while blast seizes (36:49) with no bequest (36:50), or busy enjoying in Paradise. The choice is made now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21).

**A Verse That Defines True Busyness**

Finally, this verse defines true busyness as enjoying in Paradise, not disputing in world. Worldly busyness that is entrenched in disputes (36:49) ends with no bequest and no return (36:50). Paradise busyness is enjoying that never ends, with fruits, desires, peace from Merciful Lord. The sūrah says *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead, and write what they send forth and leave behind”* (36:12) — what is sent forth determines which busyness on that Day. The believing man's rushing from farthest end (36:20) and declaration of belief (36:25) was busyness that led to being among honoured and busy enjoying. The town's busyness disputing and threatening stoning (36:18) led to being extinguished at once (36:29) and later burning.

**How Busy Enjoying Contrasts With Worldly Anxiety**

Paradise busyness contrasts with worldly anxiety because worldly busyness is often driven by fear of loss, while heavenly busyness is driven by delight in presence. In this life, people are busy securing provision, fearing drowning (36:43), fearing reversal in old age (36:68), fearing words that grieve (36:76). Their busyness is defensive. In Paradise, residents are busy enjoying themselves, with no fear, because peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) has been granted and no soul wronged (36:54). The sūrah says *“They and their spouses will be in cool shade, reclining on canopied couches”* (36:56) — reclining indicates absence of anxiety. The verse invites reader to shift busyness now from anxiety-driven disputes to gratitude-driven preparation, so that on that Day, busyness will be enjoyment, not regret. The perfect record that lists what they sent forth will show whether busyness was for disputes or for following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21). Choosing gratitude now leads to enjoyment then, while choosing dispute leads to regret, so the wise prepare early.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:56

> They and their spouses will be in ˹cool˺ shade, reclining on ˹canopied˺ couches

**Expanded Commentary**

**Shade and Couches in Paradise**

*“They and their spouses will be in cool shade, reclining on canopied couches”* (36:56) continues description of Paradise residents busy enjoying (36:55). They and their spouses, hum wa azwājuhum, indicates companionship, not solitary enjoyment. Spouses can mean wives and husbands made pure, or spouses from this world who attained Paradise, or spiritual companions particular to Paradise. The Qur'an says *“They will have spouses made pure”* (2:25, 3:15, 4:57). Shade, ẓilāl, cool shade, indicates protection from heat, comfort, rest. The sūrah earlier said night is sign where daylight stripped and they are in darkness (36:37) — darkness can be harsh, but shade in Paradise is cool, pleasant, not dark. Couches, arāʾik, canopied couches, thrones, indicate honor, rest, reclining, ittikāʾ, which is posture of contentment and peace, not anxiety. Reclining on couches in shade shows that Paradise residents are at peace, not busy with worldly concerns, but busy enjoying (36:55). The image contrasts with those in this world entrenched in disputes (36:49) and unable to make bequest (36:50) — no shade, no couches, no rest.

**Why Spouses, Shade, Couches Are Mentioned Together**

Spouses, shade, couches together indicate complete comfort: companionship, climate, furniture. Companionship removes loneliness; shade removes heat; couches remove fatigue. The three together cover social, environmental, physical comfort. The sūrah earlier mentioned gardens of palms and grapevines and springs (36:34) as worldly provision that are foretaste; shade and couches are heavenly provision that perfects worldly. The Qur'an says *“Reclining on couches, they will not see sun nor bitter cold”* (76:13) about Paradise, indicating perfect climate. Cool shade in Paradise is not like worldly shade that still has heat; it is perfect. Couches are canopied, indicating privacy and honor. The verse shows that Paradise enjoyment is not only spiritual but also physical, with spouses, shade, couches, fruits (36:57), desires, peace (36:58). The believing man made among honoured (36:27) is among those enjoying such.

**The Relation to Worldly Signs**

Shade and couches relate to worldly signs: gardens (36:34) provide shade in this world; night (36:37) provides coolness; sun and moon (36:38-40) provide light and time for rest. Paradise shade perfects worldly shade, couches perfect worldly rest. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; those who gave thanks for worldly shade and rest will have heavenly shade and couches. Those who turned away from signs (36:46) will not. The verse also contrasts with Hell: *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64) — burning, not shade; standing apart as wicked (36:59), not with spouses; no couches, no peace. The contrast shows two outcomes based on deeds recorded in perfect Record (36:12) and judged with no wrong (36:54).

**How This Verse Describes Honor**

Reclining on canopied couches indicates honor, high rank. Couches as thrones indicate that Paradise residents are honored, like the believing man made among honoured (36:27). The sūrah says *“Indeed, on that Day residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) — busy enjoying includes being honored. The Qur'an says *“They will be on couches, looking”* (83:23) about righteous, indicating they look at delights and at Lord. Looking from couches is posture of honor. The verse says they and spouses in shade reclining, indicating that honor is shared with spouses, not solitary. The sūrah's earlier promise *“good news of forgiveness and honourable reward”* (36:11) is now illustrated: forgiveness leads to Paradise, honourable reward includes spouses, shade, couches. The reward is honourable, karīm, generous, dignified, given by generous host.

**A Verse That Invites Aspiration**

This verse invites aspiration to be among those in shade reclining on couches. It is short, but it holds image of comfort that is universally desired: cool shade, comfortable couch, beloved spouse, no anxiety. The image is accessible, not abstract, so reader can imagine and desire. The sūrah says *“Therein they have fruit and whatsoever they call for”* (36:57) next, adding fruits and desires, and *“Peace will be their greeting from Merciful Lord”* (36:58) as ultimate greeting. Shade and couches are part of that progression: from busy enjoying (36:55) to shade and couches (36:56) to fruits and desires (36:57) to peace from Lord (36:58). The progression moves from general enjoyment to specific comforts to ultimate greeting. The verse is middle step, showing that Paradise provides both physical and social comfort, and that following Reminder (36:11), following those who ask no reward (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22) leads to such comfort, while turning away (36:46) and refusing to donate (36:47) leads to burning.

**The Link to Return and Authority**

Finally, shade and couches in Paradise are under authority of One in Whose Hands is authority over all things and to Whom all will be returned (36:83). The One who created pairs (36:36), night and day, sun and moon, gardens and springs, also creates Paradise with shade and couches. The verse shows that authority includes ability to create perfect comfort for those who believed. The reader who is now in worldly shade, perhaps under tree or building, is invited to see shade as sign pointing to cool shade in Paradise, and to give thanks now so that he may be among those reclining in shade then, with spouses, on canopied couches, busy enjoying, having fruits and whatever they desire, greeted with peace from Merciful Lord.

**Shade as Mercy That Invites Reflection**

Cool shade in Paradise perfects worldly shade that was sign. In this world, shade provides relief from sun that travels for fixed term (36:38), and night provides rest after daylight stripped (36:37). Both are temporary and by mercy allowing enjoyment for little while (36:44). In Paradise, shade is permanent, cool, without harshness, and couches are canopied, indicating honor and privacy. The sūrah says residents busy enjoying (36:55) will be in shade reclining, showing that shade is part of enjoyment, not separate. The believing man made among honoured (36:27) enjoys such shade, while his killers face burning (36:64) with no shade. The verse invites to give thanks for worldly shade now and to seek heavenly shade by following Reminder and worshiping Originator.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:57

> There they will have fruits and whatever they desire

**Expanded Commentary**

**Fruits and Desires in Paradise**

*“There they will have fruits and whatever they desire”* (36:57) continues Paradise description after busy enjoying (36:55) and shade and couches with spouses (36:56). Fruits, fākihah, indicates delights, not just food but enjoyment. The sūrah earlier mentioned grain for eating (36:33), gardens of palms and grapevines and springs for fruit (36:34-35), and cattle benefits and drinks (36:72-73). Paradise fruits perfect those worldly fruits, without toil, without seasonal limit, without decay. The Qur'an says *“Every time they are provided with fruit therefrom as provision, they will say This is what we were provided before, and they will be given similar”* (2:25) — similar in name but superior in taste. Whatever they desire, mā yaddaʿūn, whatever they call for, claim, wish, indicates that Paradise provides not only fruits but anything desired. The Qur'an says *“They will have whatever they wish with their Lord”* (25:16, 39:34, 41:31, 42:22) and *“In it is whatever souls desire and eyes delight in”* (43:71). The verse thus promises abundance beyond fruits: whatever desired is granted.

**Why Fruits and Desires Are Paired**

Fruits and whatever they desire are paired to show that Paradise provides both specific — fruits — and general — whatever desired. Fruits are example of provision that is tangible, familiar, delightful; whatever they desire is general promise that covers all wishes. The pairing shows that Paradise is not limited to fruits; it includes all desires. The sūrah says *“Indeed, on that Day residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) — busy enjoying includes fruits and desires. The verse says there, fīhā, therein, in Paradise, indicating location of fruits and desires. The worldly gardens and springs (36:34) were in earth, fīhā, therein, earth; Paradise fruits are therein, Paradise, showing parallel but superior location. The question *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for worldly fruits should lead to thanks that leads to heavenly fruits. Those who gave thanks for worldly fruits will have heavenly fruits and whatever they desire.

**The Relation to Worldly Provision**

Worldly provision — grain, gardens, springs, pairs, night, sun, moon, Ark, ships, cattle — all are signs that should lead to thanks and that are foretastes of Paradise provision. Fruits in Paradise perfect worldly fruits: worldly fruits require planting, watering, waiting, and can rot; heavenly fruits require no toil, no waiting, no rot. Whatever they desire in Paradise perfects worldly desires that are often unfulfilled or lead to harm. In Paradise, desires are purified and fulfilled without harm. The sūrah says *“They derive from them other benefits and drinks. Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:73) about cattle, showing that worldly benefits should lead to thanks. Paradise benefits are greater, so thanks should be greater. The believing man made among honoured (36:27) is among those having fruits and whatever they desire, because he gave thanks by following messengers and worshiping Originator.

**How This Verse Describes Abundance**

This verse describes abundance: fruits and whatever they desire. Abundance is characteristic of Paradise, where there is no scarcity, no competition, no envy. The Qur'an says *“No soul knows what is hidden for them of comfort as reward for what they used to do”* (32:17). Fruits and whatever they desire is part of hidden comfort. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54), so abundance is reward for deeds, not arbitrary. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth determines abundance. Those who sent forth following Reminder (36:11), donating from provision (36:47), worshiping Originator (36:22) will have abundance; those who turned away (36:46) will have *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64), no fruits, no desires fulfilled, but barrier between them and what they desire (34:54).

**A Verse That Invites Aspiration and Trust**

This verse invites aspiration to have fruits and whatever desired in Paradise, and trust that One who created fruits in this world without human hands (36:35) can create fruits in next world without toil. The sūrah says *“So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is authority over all things, and to Whom you will all be returned”* (36:83) — authority over all things includes authority to provide fruits and fulfill desires. The reader who desires fruits and other things now is invited to desire them from God in Paradise, not just in world, and to prepare by giving thanks, following Reminder, following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21). The verse is short, but it holds promise of abundance that is both familiar — fruits — and open-ended — whatever they desire — showing that Paradise exceeds imagination while including familiar delights.

**The Link to Peace From Merciful Lord**

Finally, fruits and whatever they desire are followed by ultimate greeting: *“Peace will be their greeting from Merciful Lord”* (36:58). Fruits and desires are provision; peace from Lord is greeting that makes provision enjoyable. Without peace, fruits would not be enjoyed; with peace, even simple fruit is delightful. The sūrah's progression from busy enjoying (36:55) to shade and couches (36:56) to fruits and desires (36:57) to peace from Lord (36:58) shows that Paradise enjoyment culminates in divine greeting, not just material. The verse invites reader to see fruits now as sign pointing to fruits then, and to see whatever desired now as sign that true fulfillment is with Lord who can grant whatever desired in Paradise, where no soul wronged and reward is based on what was used to do, and where authority over all things ensures that fruits and desires are granted without limit. Such abundance should motivate gratitude today and hope for tomorrow, turning daily eating into remembrance of the Giver.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:58

> And “Peace!” will be ˹their˺ greeting from the Merciful Lord

**Expanded Commentary**

**Peace as Greeting From Merciful Lord**

*“And Peace will be their greeting from the Merciful Lord”* (36:58) culminates Paradise description after busy enjoying (36:55), shade and couches with spouses (36:56), fruits and whatever desired (36:57). Salām, peace, is greeting, word, from Lord Merciful, Rab Raḥīm. The sūrah earlier said revelation is from Almighty, Most Merciful (36:5) — al-ʿAzīz al-Raḥīm — and now greeting is from Lord Most Merciful, Rab Raḥīm, showing that mercy that sent revelation now greets with peace. The Qur'an says *“Their greeting on Day they meet Him will be Peace”* (33:44) and *“Peace, a word from Merciful Lord”* is similar. Peace as greeting means safety from all harm, fear, grief, and includes wholeness, soundness. The Lord who is Most Merciful greets with peace, indicating that Paradise residents are safe from punishment, from loss of provision, from end of enjoyment. The greeting is from Lord, not from angels only, though angels also greet with peace, but ultimate greeting is from Lord Himself, which is highest honor.

**Why Peace Is Ultimate Greeting**

Peace is ultimate greeting because it encompasses all good. If one has peace from Lord, one has safety from Hell, from wrong (36:54), from being extinguished (36:29), from being seized while disputing (36:49), from having no bequest or return (36:50). Peace is what believing man wished his people knew: forgiveness and being among honoured (36:27) — forgiveness is peace from punishment, honor is peace from disgrace. The sūrah says *“Indeed, on that Day residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) — enjoyment is possible because peace is granted. Without peace, enjoyment would be anxious. The greeting of peace from Merciful Lord ensures that enjoyment is secure. The Qur'an says *“For them is abode of peace with their Lord”* (6:127). Abode of peace is Paradise, and greeting of peace is from Lord of that abode. The verse is short, but it holds culmination of mercy: from revelation from Merciful (36:5) to greeting of peace from Merciful.

**How Peace Relates to Previous Paradise Descriptions**

Peace as greeting follows fruits and whatever desired (36:57) because fruits and desires are enjoyed in peace. Shade and couches (36:56) are enjoyed in peace. Busy enjoying (36:55) is possible in peace. The progression shows that material delights — shade, couches, spouses, fruits, desires — are crowned by spiritual delight — peace from Lord. The sūrah says *“They and their spouses will be in cool shade, reclining on canopied couches”* (36:56) — shade and couches are physical; peace from Lord is spiritual that makes physical enjoyable. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and made among honoured (36:27) is among those greeted with peace. His wish *“If only my people knew how my Lord forgave me and made me among honoured”* (36:26-27) is wish that they knew forgiveness and honor that lead to peace from Lord. The verse thus completes his story: he entered Paradise, is among honoured, has fruits and desires, and is greeted with peace.

**The Contrast With Hell's Greeting**

Peace from Merciful Lord contrasts with Hell's address: *“Step away this Day, O wicked ones! Did I not command you not to follow Satan? This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:59-64). One group greeted with peace, other told to step away and burn. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54), so both greetings are just. The difference is based on what they used to do (36:54) — following Reminder and fearing unseen (36:11) leads to peace; turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48) leads to step away and burn. The verse invites reader to choose which greeting he wants to hear on that Day: peace from Merciful Lord or step away and burn. The choice is made now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21) and worshiping Originator (36:22).

**A Verse That Defines True Success**

This verse defines true success as receiving peace from Merciful Lord. Worldly success — wealth, status, being able to make bequest and return to people — ends with no bequest and no return when blast seizes (36:50). True success is peace from Lord that never ends. The Qur'an says *“Whoever is removed from Fire and admitted to Garden has indeed succeeded”* (3:185). Peace from Lord is sign of that success. The sūrah says *“So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is authority over all things, and to Whom you will all be returned”* (36:83) — return to Him can be to peace or to burning, depending on deeds. The verse is short, but it holds ultimate hope: that Lord who is Most Merciful, who sent revelation from Almighty, Most Merciful (36:5), who allows enjoyment for little while by mercy (36:44), will greet with peace those who followed and feared. The reader is invited to seek that greeting now by giving thanks for provision (36:35, 36:73), donating, following Reminder, and preparing for Day when all will be brought before Him (36:32, 36:53).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, peace from Merciful Lord shows that authority over all things (36:83) includes authority to grant peace. The One who could drown them with no responder (36:43) and who could blind eyes and transfigure on spot (36:66-67) chooses to greet with peace those who believed. The choice is mercy. The verse is placed after Paradise fruits and desires and before Hell separation, showing that peace is dividing line. Those who receive peace are in Paradise busy enjoying; those who do not are told to step away. The verse invites reader to imagine hearing peace from Lord, to desire it, and to live now in way that leads to hearing it then — by following Reminder, fearing unseen, worshiping Originator, rejecting powerless intercessors, giving thanks, and seeking mercy while little while remains.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:59

> ˹Then the disbelievers will be told,˺ “Step away ˹from the believers˺ this Day, O wicked ones

**Expanded Commentary**

**Separation of Wicked on Day of Judgment**

*“Then the disbelievers will be told, Step away from the believers this Day, O wicked ones”* (36:59) begins Hell section after Paradise peace (36:58). The command imtāzū, step away, separate, distinguish yourselves, indicates division of humanity on Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53). The Qur'an calls Day of Judgment Day of Division, yawm al-faṣl (37:21, 44:40, 77:13-14, 78:17), when believers and disbelievers separated. The sūrah earlier said *“Oh pity, such beings! No messenger ever came to them without being mocked”* (36:30) and *“Have they not considered how many peoples We destroyed who never came back?”* (36:31) — mockers and destroyed will be separated from believers. The address O wicked ones, mujrimūn, criminals, guilty, indicates those who committed crimes of turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48), taking other gods (36:74). Separation is first step of judgment: wicked told to step away from believers, so that believers can enjoy Paradise busy enjoying (36:55) without presence of wicked, and wicked can face Hell they were warned of (36:63).

**Why Separation Is Necessary**

Separation is necessary because Paradise and Hell are distinct abodes with distinct inhabitants. The sūrah says *“Indeed, on that Day residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) and *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64) — two abodes, two groups. Mixing would be injustice; separation is justice. The Qur'an says *“On Day when faces will be whitened and faces blackened”* (3:106) — whitened and blackened separated. The command step away is from God, showing authority over all things (36:83). The wicked who said *“You will be touched with painful punishment from us”* (36:18), claiming authority to punish, are now told to step away by true authority. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and made among honoured (36:27) is among those from whom wicked step away. His wish *“If only my people knew”* (36:26-27) is now answered by separation: his people who killed him are among wicked told to step away, while he is among honoured in Paradise.

**How Separation Happens**

Separation happens by divine command, not by human choice. The wicked cannot remain among believers; they must step away. The Qur'an says *“On Day when We will gather them all, then We will say to those who associated others, Stay in your place, you and your partners”* (10:28) — similar command to stay in place, separate. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54), so separation is just, based on perfect Record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth and left behind. The wicked's record shows turning away, refusing, mocking, taking other gods, so they are told to step away. The believers' record shows following Reminder (36:11), following those who ask no reward (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22), giving thanks (36:35, 36:73), so they remain in Paradise. The separation is thus based on deeds, not arbitrary.

**The Relation to Previous Paradise Description**

This verse follows peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) to show contrast: one group greeted with peace, other told to step away. The sūrah's structure moves from Paradise busy enjoying (36:55-58) to Hell separation and burning (36:59-64), showing two outcomes side by side so reader can choose. The verse is short, but it holds complete division: believers in shade reclining on couches with spouses having fruits and desires greeted with peace; wicked told to step away as criminals. The contrast is intentional and stark. The reader who is now in worldly life, perhaps mixing with both, is told that mixing is temporary; Day will separate. The Qur'an says *“The companions of Garden and companions of Fire are not equal; companions of Garden are the successful”* (59:20). Separation makes inequality manifest.

**A Verse That Defines Wickedness**

This verse defines wickedness as being among those told to step away. Wicked, mujrim, is one who commits jurm, crime, which includes disbelief, turning away from signs, refusing to donate, mocking threat, taking other gods, following Satan (36:60). The sūrah says *“Children of Adam were commanded not to follow Satan, their clear enemy, but to worship Me alone? This is the Straight Path”* (36:60-61) next, explaining that wickedness is following Satan instead of straight path. The command step away is thus consequence of following Satan. The verse invites reader to examine whether he is following Satan or straight path, and to choose straight path now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), before being told to step away on Day when no bequest or return possible (36:50).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, step away from believers this Day, O wicked ones, shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. The One who could drown them with no responder (36:43) and who allows enjoyment for little while by mercy (36:44) now commands separation. The command is obeyed, because authority is His. The verse is short, but it holds complete eschatology of division that is just, based on record, and that leads to Paradise peace or Hell burning. The reader is invited to be among those who will not be told to step away, but among those busy enjoying, reclining in shade, having fruits and whatever desired, greeted with peace from Merciful Lord, by following Reminder, fearing unseen, worshiping Originator, giving thanks, donating, and seeking mercy while little while remains.

**Separation as Justice That Clarifies Outcomes**

Step away this Day, O wicked ones, is command that clarifies outcomes after Paradise peace. The sūrah has shown Paradise busy enjoying with shade, couches, fruits, desires, peace (36:55-58), then says wicked must step away, so that two groups not mixed. Separation is justice because perfect record (36:12) distinguishes deeds, and no soul wronged (36:54) requires distinction. The town extinguished at once (36:29) and past peoples destroyed who never came back (36:31) will be among those told to step away, while believing man who rushed and declared belief (36:20-25) and was told Enter Paradise (36:26) remains with honoured. The verse invites reader to consider which group he will be in when separation commanded, and to choose now to be among those who will not be told to step away.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:60

> Did I not command you, O Children of Adam, not to follow Satan, for he is truly your sworn enemy

**Expanded Commentary**

**Command Not to Follow Satan**

*“Did I not command you, O Children of Adam, not to follow Satan, for he is truly your sworn enemy”* (36:60) is address to wicked who were told to step away (36:59), reminding them of command given in world. The question form a-lam aʿhad ilaykum, did I not enjoin upon you, did I not command you, indicates covenant, pledge, taken from Children of Adam. The Book states *“And when your Lord took from Children of Adam, from their loins, their descendants and made them testify: Am I not your Lord? They said Yes”* (7:172) — primordial covenant. Command not to follow Satan is part of that covenant and repeated through messengers. Satan, shayṭān, is described as sworn enemy, ʿaduww mubīn, manifest enemy, enemy whose enmity is clear, evident. Scripture notes *“Indeed, Satan is enemy to you, so take him as enemy”* (35:6). The sūrah says *“Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense?”* (36:62) next, showing that following Satan leads to being misled. The command not to follow Satan is thus foundational.

**Why Children of Adam Are Addressed**

Children of Adam, banī Ādam, indicates all humanity, descendants of Adam, not just specific town. The command not to follow Satan was given to all, through Adam's story where Satan refused to prostrate and vowed to mislead, and through messengers who warned against following Satan. The sūrah earlier told story of town that rejected messengers (36:13-29) and of people who turn away from signs (36:46), refuse to donate (36:47), mock threat (36:48) — all are forms of following Satan, who whispers to turn away, to be miserly, to mock. The address O Children of Adam universalizes: all were commanded, not just that town. It is reported *“O Children of Adam, let not Satan tempt you as he removed your parents from Garden”* (7:27). The temptation that removed parents from Garden was following Satan's whisper to eat from tree; following Satan still removes from Garden, now Paradise. The verse reminds that enmity of Satan is manifest, not hidden, so following him is inexcusable.

**Satan as Sworn Enemy**

Satan as sworn enemy, ʿaduww mubīn, means his enmity is clear, evident, not secret. The Qur'an says *“Satan is enemy to you, so take him as enemy. He only invites his party to be among companions of Blaze”* (35:6). His invitation is to follow him to Blaze, which is Hell. The sūrah says *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64) — Hell is destination of Satan's party. The command not to follow Satan is thus command not to follow path to Hell. The sūrah says *“but to worship Me alone? This is the Straight Path”* (36:61) next, contrasting following Satan with worshiping God alone, which is straight path. Following Satan is deviation, error, manifest error (36:24). The believing man who said *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me, and to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) and *“How could I take besides Him other gods?”* (36:23) followed straight path, not Satan. His reward was *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and being among honoured (36:27).

**How Command Was Given**

Command not to follow Satan was given through primordial covenant (7:172), through Adam's story, through messengers, through signs. The sūrah says *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen”* (36:11) — Reminder includes command not to follow Satan. The sūrah says *“We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting for him. This Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an”* (36:69) — Reminder includes command. The town that rejected messengers (36:13-18) and threatened stoning (36:18) followed Satan, who is sworn enemy, not messengers who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21). The sūrah says *“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75) — serving idols is following Satan, who invites to idolatry. The command not to follow Satan is thus central to sūrah's message.

**A Verse That Defines Enmity**

This verse defines enmity: Satan is truly sworn enemy. Enmity is not hidden; it is manifest. The Qur'an says *“Satan promises you poverty and commands you to immorality, while God promises you forgiveness and bounty”* (2:268). The contrast shows enmity: Satan commands immorality and miserliness — like refusing to donate (36:47) — while God commands forgiveness and bounty. Following Satan leads to being among wicked told to step away (36:59); following God leads to being among those busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) greeted with peace (36:58). The verse invites reader to recognize Satan's enmity and to not follow him, but to worship God alone, which is straight path (36:61). The question did I not command you implies that command was given and is known, so following Satan is disobedience to known command, not ignorance.

**The Link to Straight Path and Authority**

Finally, command not to follow Satan and to worship God alone as straight path (36:60-61) shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. Following Satan is following one who has no authority, who cannot help (36:75), whose intercession does not benefit (36:23). Worshiping God alone is following One who has authority over all things, who carries in Ark and ships (36:41-42), who could drown but allows enjoyment by mercy (36:43-44), who resurrects dead and writes what they send forth (36:12), who will bring all before Him (36:32, 36:53) with no soul wronged (36:54). The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of enmity and command, inviting reader to obey command not to follow manifest enemy but to worship Merciful Lord who greets with peace, before being told to step away as wicked.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:61

> but to worship Me ˹alone˺? This is the Straight Path

**Expanded Commentary**

**Worship Me Alone as Straight Path**

*“But to worship Me alone? This is the Straight Path”* (36:61) continues command not to follow Satan (36:60) with positive command: worship Me alone, and statement that this is straight path. The sūrah earlier said messenger is upon straight path (36:4) — *“upon the Straight Path”* (36:4) — and now says worshiping God alone is straight path, linking messenger's path to worship. The Qur'an says *“Indeed, God is my Lord and your Lord, so worship Him. This is straight path”* (3:51, 19:36). Straight path, ṣirāṭ mustaqīm, is broad, clear road, shortest distance between human and divine pleasure, without crookedness. The sūrah says *“Did I not command you, O Children of Adam, not to follow Satan, for he is truly your sworn enemy, but to worship Me alone? This is the Straight Path”* (36:60-61) — two commands: not follow Satan, worship God alone, and this — worshiping God alone, not following Satan — is straight path. The believing man who said *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me, and to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) and *“How could I take besides Him other gods?”* (36:23) followed straight path and was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26).

**Why Worship Me Alone Is Straight Path**

Worshiping God alone is straight path because it aligns with fiṭrah, primordial nature upon which God originated mankind (30:30), with origin and return (36:22), with provision that hands did not make (36:35), with pairs created (36:36), with night and day, sun and moon ordered (36:37-40), with Ark and ships carried (36:41-42), with cattle subjected (36:71-72). All signs point to One Creator, so worshiping One is straight, consistent with reality; worshiping others is crooked, inconsistent. The sūrah says *“They have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped by them. They cannot help”* (36:74-75) — taking other gods is not straight because they cannot help. The sūrah says *“Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense?”* (36:62) about Satan misleading from straight path. Following Satan is deviation; worshiping God alone is straight. The verse is short, but it defines straight path as worshiping God alone, not following Satan.

**The Relation to Earlier Straight Path**

The sūrah opened with *“You are truly one of the messengers upon the Straight Path”* (36:3-4) — messenger upon straight path — and now says worshiping God alone is straight path, showing that messenger's path is worshiping God alone and calling others to same. The sūrah says *“We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting for him. This Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an”* (36:69) — Reminder calls to straight path. The town that rejected messengers (36:13-18) rejected straight path; the believing man who followed messengers (36:20-21) followed straight path. The verse thus links opening and middle and end: straight path is consistent throughout. The Qur'an says *“Guide us to Straight Path”* (1:6) — prayer answered by *“You are upon Straight Path”* (36:4) and *“This is Straight Path”* (36:61) — worship God alone.

**How This Verse Defines Worship**

Worship, ʿibādah, in Qur'anic usage includes prayer, charity, gratitude, obedience, love, fear, hope. Worshiping God alone means directing all those to God, not to idols, Satan, or others. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) — thanks is part of worship. Donating from what Allah provided (36:47) is part of worship. Following Reminder (36:11) is part of worship. The verse says but to worship Me alone, an iʿbudūnī, worship Me, with alone implied, indicating exclusivity. The Qur'an says *“I did not create jinn and mankind except to worship Me”* (51:56) — purpose of creation is worship alone. The believing man's question *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me, and to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) shows that worship is response to origination and return. Worshiping Originator to Whom return is straight path because it aligns with beginning and end.

**A Verse That Invites to Straight Path**

This verse invites to straight path by stating it plainly: worship Me alone, this is straight path. It is invitation, not compulsion, because messenger's duty is only clear delivery (36:17). The sūrah says *“So do not let their words grieve you. Indeed, We fully know what they conceal and reveal”* (36:76) — grieving words include mocking straight path. The verse is consolation that straight path is clear and defined, regardless of mocking. The reader who is now in worldly life, perhaps following Satan in some ways — turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48) — is invited to leave that and worship God alone, which is straight path. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) — following straight path leads to no wrong, to busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58); following Satan leads to step away as wicked (36:59) and burn in Hell (36:63-64).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, worship Me alone as straight path shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. Worshiping One who has authority and to Whom return is, is straight because it aligns with reality of authority and return. Worshiping others who have no authority and cannot help (36:75) is crooked. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of worship as straight path, inviting reader to worship God alone now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), before being brought before Him (36:32, 36:53) and told either peace (36:58) or burn (36:64), based on whether he followed straight path or followed sworn enemy.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:62

> Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense

**Expanded Commentary**

**Satan Misled Great Multitudes**

*“Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense?”* (36:62) follows command not to follow Satan and to worship God alone as straight path (36:60-61) with statement that Satan already misled great multitudes, jibillan kathīran, large groups, generations, and question did you not have sense, a-fa-lam takūnū taʿqilūn, did you not use intellect. The verse indicates that following Satan is not isolated but has led many astray, and that intellect should have prevented it. The Book states *“Satan is enemy to you, so take him as enemy. He only invites his party to be among companions of Blaze”* (35:6) and *“Did I not command you not to follow Satan?”* (36:60). Despite command and clear enmity, great multitudes followed, showing that enmity being manifest (36:60) does not prevent following if sense not used. The sūrah earlier described barriers before and behind and covering so they fail to see truth (36:9) and turning away whenever sign comes (36:46) — not using sense leads to being misled.

**Why Great Multitudes Matters**

Great multitudes, jibillan kathīran, indicates that following Satan is common, not rare. The town that rejected messengers (36:13-18) and threatened stoning (36:18) was multitude; past peoples destroyed who never came back (36:31) were multitudes; those who turn away from signs (36:46), refuse to donate (36:47), mock threat (36:48) are multitudes. The verse says yet he already misled great multitudes of you, addressing present audience as part of same pattern. Scripture notes *“If you obey most of those on earth, they will lead you away from God's path”* (6:116) — most can be misled. The fact that many follow Satan does not make following right; it makes it common. The believing man who rushed from farthest end (36:20) and declared belief (36:25) was alone against multitude, yet he was guided and told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26). One guided against multitude shows that truth is not measured by numbers.

**Did You Not Have Any Sense as Rebuke**

Did you not have any sense, a-fa-lam takūnū taʿqilūn, is rebuke that intellect was not used. ʿAql, sense, intellect, in Qur'anic usage is faculty that restrains, that binds, that prevents from following desires and Satan. It is reported *“We have certainly created many of jinn and mankind for Hell, having hearts with which they do not understand, eyes with which they do not see, ears with which they do not hear; they are like cattle, rather more astray”* (7:179). Not using sense makes like cattle. The sūrah says *“And whoever We grant long life, We reverse them in development. Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) — will they not use sense to see that long life leads to reversal, so this life is not permanent. The question did you not have sense invites to use sense now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), to see that Satan misled great multitudes and to not be among them.

**How Satan Misleads**

Satan misleads by whispering, beautifying, promising, commanding. The Qur'an says *“Satan promises you poverty and commands you to immorality”* (2:268) — promising poverty leads to refusing to donate (36:47); commanding immorality leads to mocking, turning away. Satan says *“I will surely mislead them and give them false hopes”* (4:119). False hopes include that threat will not come (36:48) and that idols will help (36:74-75). The sūrah says *“They have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped by them”* (36:74) — hoping to be helped by idols is from Satan's false hopes. The verse says he already misled great multitudes, indicating that misleading is past and ongoing. The present audience is among those multitudes if they follow same pattern. The verse invites to break pattern by using sense, following Reminder (36:11), following those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22).

**The Relation to Straight Path**

This verse follows *“but to worship Me alone? This is the Straight Path”* (36:61) to show that many deviated from straight path by following Satan. Straight path is worshiping God alone; deviation is following Satan. The sūrah opened with messenger upon straight path (36:4) and now says many misled from it. The contrast shows that straight path remains straight even if many leave it. The Qur'an says *“This is My straight path, so follow it, and do not follow other paths, for they will separate you from His path”* (6:153). Other paths are Satan's paths. The verse says did you not have sense, implying sense should have kept on straight path. The believing man had sense: he asked *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me?”* (36:22) — using sense to see origination and return, so he stayed on straight path. His people did not use sense, so they were among multitudes misled and extinguished at once (36:29).

**A Verse That Warns Against Following Majority**

Finally, this verse warns against following majority who are misled. Great multitudes misled does not justify following; it warns. The Qur'an says *“Most of them do not believe”* (2:100) and *“Most of them do not know”* (6:37) — majority can be wrong. The verse invites to be among few who use sense, not among multitudes misled. The sūrah says *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen”* (36:11) — those are few who use sense. The perfect record (36:12) will show whether one was among multitudes misled or among few guided, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that. The verse is short, but it holds complete warning: Satan already misled great multitudes, did you not have sense? Use sense now, while little while remains (36:44), to follow straight path of worshiping God alone, before being told *“This is Hell you were warned of. Burn in it today”* (36:63-64).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:63

> This is the Hell you were warned of

**Expanded Commentary**

**Hell Shown to Those Who Followed Satan**

*“This is the Hell you were warned of”* (36:63) is address to wicked who were told to step away (36:59), reminded of command not to follow Satan (36:60-61), and told Satan misled great multitudes (36:62). Now Hell is shown: hādhihi jahannam allatī kuntum tūʿadūn, this is Hell you were promised, warned of. The demonstrative hādhihi, this, indicates Hell is present, visible, pointed to, after being unseen threat that was mocked: *“When will this threat come, if you are truthful?”* (36:48). Now threat has come as Hell present. The sūrah earlier said *“They must be awaiting a single Blast, which will seize them while they are entrenched in worldly disputes”* (36:49) and *“The Trumpet will be blown, then behold, they will rush from graves to their Lord”* (36:51) — Hell is destination after rushing. The verse says you were warned of, kuntum tūʿadūn, you were promised, warned, indicating that messengers warned, Reminder warned, signs warned, but they turned away (36:46). Now warning is realized as Hell present.

**Why This Is Hell You Were Warned Of Matters**

Saying this is Hell you were warned of matters because it shows that warning was true and that Hell is not new but was warned of before. The sūrah says *“They will cry, Woe to us! Who has raised us from our place of rest? This must be what the Most Compassionate warned us of; the messengers told the truth”* (36:52) — they themselves will acknowledge that what they see is what Most Compassionate warned of and messengers told truth about. This verse is that acknowledgment from divine side: this is Hell you were warned of. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth includes warnings given and ignored, and now Hell is recompense for ignoring. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) — Hell is reward for what they used to do — turning away, refusing to donate, mocking, taking other gods, following Satan. The verse is short, but it holds principle that Hell is not arbitrary but was warned of, so those entering cannot claim surprise.

**The Relation to Paradise Description**

This verse follows Paradise description — busy enjoying (36:55), shade and couches with spouses (36:56), fruits and whatever desired (36:57), peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) — and separation of wicked (36:59) and reminder of command (36:60-62), to show contrast: Paradise for those who followed Reminder and feared unseen (36:11), Hell for those who followed Satan and were among multitudes misled (36:62). The sūrah's structure is Paradise then Hell, so reader can compare and choose. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and made among honoured (36:27) is among Paradise residents busy enjoying; his killers who threatened stoning (36:18) and were extinguished at once (36:29) are among those told this is Hell you were warned of. The contrast is intentional: one group wished his people knew forgiveness and honor (36:26-27), other group now sees Hell they were warned of.

**How This Verse Connects to Authority**

This is Hell you were warned of shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and that He warned out of mercy, allowing enjoyment for little while (36:44) before Hell. The sūrah says *“If We willed, We could drown them, then no one would respond to their cries, nor would they be rescued, except by mercy from Us, allowing them enjoyment for a little while”* (36:43-44) — mercy allowed enjoyment, warning allowed preparation, but they turned away. Now Hell is shown as consequence. The verse also shows that warning was from Most Compassionate (36:52) — Most Compassionate warned of Hell out of mercy, not cruelty, so that people might avoid it. Those who ignored warning now see Hell they were warned of. The verse invites reader to heed warning now, while little while remains, by following straight path of worshiping God alone (36:61), which leads to peace from Merciful Lord (36:58), not to Hell.

**A Verse That Defines Warning as Mercy**

Finally, this verse defines warning as mercy: Hell you were warned of was warned of so you might avoid it. The sūrah opened with revelation from Almighty, Most Merciful (36:5) so that you may warn a people whose forefathers were not warned and so are heedless (36:6). Warning heedless is mercy. The town that rejected messengers (36:13-18) was warned by messengers who said our duty is only clear delivery (36:17); they ignored warning and were extinguished (36:29). Mecca that mocked messenger (36:30) was warned by wise Qur'an (36:2) and signs (36:33-44); if it ignores, Hell it was warned of awaits. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of warning: Hell exists, it was warned of by Most Compassionate through messengers who told truth, and seeing it now is confirmation that warning was true. The reader is invited to believe warning now, before seeing Hell, so that he will be among those busy enjoying in Paradise, not among those told this is Hell you were warned of and burn in it today (36:64).

**Hell You Were Warned Of as Real Destination**

This is Hell you were warned of is statement that Hell is real, present, and was warned of before, not invented on Day. The sūrah says Most Compassionate warned (36:52) and messengers told truth (36:52), and now Hell shown as confirmation. The town extinguished at once (36:29) and past peoples destroyed (36:31) are examples of punishment before, and Hell is final punishment after. The perfect record (36:12) that lists warnings ignored and deeds done will be basis for being shown Hell. The verse invites to heed warning now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following straight path of worshiping God alone (36:61), so that one will not be shown Hell you were warned of but will be among those greeted with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:64

> Burn in it Today for your disbelief.”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Command to Burn in Hell for Disbelief**

*“Burn in it today for your disbelief”* (36:64) follows *“This is Hell you were warned of”* (36:63) with command to enter and burn: iṣlawhā al-yawm bimā kuntum takfurūn, burn in it today for what you used to disbelieve. The command is from God, showing authority over all things (36:83). Today, al-yawm, indicates Day of Resurrection, Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53), Day when no soul wronged (36:54). Burning is recompense for disbelief, kufr, which includes turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48), taking other gods (36:74), following Satan (36:60-62). The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) — burning is reward for what they used to do — disbelief. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth includes disbelief, and now burning is based on that record. The verse is short, but it holds finality: burn today, no delay, for disbelief.

**Why Today and For Disbelief Are Emphasized**

Today emphasizes immediacy: after being shown Hell you were warned of (36:63), command is burn today, no postponement. The sūrah earlier said *“They must be awaiting a single Blast, which will seize them while they are entrenched in worldly disputes. Then they will not be able to make a last will, nor can they return to their own people”* (36:49-50) — no time for bequest or return at first blast; similarly, no time to delay burning at second. For your disbelief, bimā kuntum takfurūn, gives reason: because you used to disbelieve. The sūrah says *“Have the deniers not considered how many peoples We destroyed before them who never came back?”* (36:31) — destruction was for disbelief; burning is also for disbelief. The believing man who declared *“I believe in your Lord, so listen to me”* (36:25) and was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and made among honoured (36:27) is contrast: he believed, so entry into Paradise; they disbelieved, so burn in Hell. The reason for burning is disbelief, not arbitrary.

**The Relation to Paradise Peace**

Burn in it today for disbelief contrasts with *“Peace will be their greeting from Merciful Lord”* (36:58) for Paradise residents. One group greeted with peace, other commanded to burn. The sūrah says *“Indeed, on that Day residents of Paradise will be busy enjoying themselves”* (36:55) — busy enjoying vs burning. The contrast shows two outcomes based on belief vs disbelief. The sūrah says *“They and their spouses will be in cool shade, reclining on canopied couches”* (36:56) — shade and couches vs burning. *“There they will have fruits and whatever they desire”* (36:57) — fruits and desires vs burning. The verse invites reader to choose which command he wants to hear: peace from Merciful Lord or burn in Hell today for disbelief. The choice is made now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following Reminder (36:11), following those who ask no reward (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22).

**How This Verse Connects to Authority and Mercy**

Burn in Hell today for disbelief shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and that He warned out of mercy (36:5, 36:44) before burning. The sūrah says *“If We willed, We could drown them, then no one would respond to their cries, nor would they be rescued, except by mercy from Us, allowing them enjoyment for a little while”* (36:43-44) — mercy allowed enjoyment, warning allowed preparation, but disbelief persisted, so burning today. The verse also shows that Hell is not for minor faults but for disbelief — persistent rejection of signs, messengers, Reminder. The sūrah says *“Whenever a sign comes to them from their Lord, they turn away from it”* (36:46) — turning away is disbelief. The perfect record (36:12) records disbelief, and burning is based on record, so no soul wronged (36:54).

**A Verse That Defines Consequence**
