Finally, this verse defines consequence of disbelief as burning in Hell today. It is not metaphor but real burning, as sūrah says *“This is Hell you were warned of”* (36:63) — Hell is real, warned of, now present, and command is burn in it. The Qur'an says *“Indeed, Hell is lying in ambush, for the transgressors a destination”* (78:21-22). The town that threatened *“you will be touched with painful punishment from us”* (36:18) now touches painful punishment from God, not from town. The reversal is just. The verse is short, but it holds finality and justice: burn today for disbelief, after being warned, after being given signs, after being allowed enjoyment for little while by mercy. The reader is invited to avoid disbelief now, to believe in Lord of messengers (36:25), to worship Originator (36:22), to give thanks (36:35, 36:73), to seek peace from Merciful Lord (36:58), before being told burn in it today for disbelief.

**Burning Today as Immediate Recompense**

Burn in it today for disbelief shows immediacy: today, Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53), Day when no soul wronged (36:54), burning is recompense for disbelief. The sūrah says town extinguished at once by single blast (36:29) and those seized while disputing cannot make bequest or return (36:49-50), showing suddenness. Burning today is similar suddenness after being shown Hell (36:63). The reason for burning is disbelief — turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48), taking other gods hoping to be helped (36:74) that cannot help (36:75). The believing man who believed in Lord of messengers (36:25) and was told Enter Paradise (36:26) is contrast. The verse invites to avoid disbelief now, to believe, worship Originator, give thanks, before being told burn today.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:65

> On this Day We will seal their mouths, their hands will speak to Us, and their feet will testify to what they used to commit

**Expanded Commentary**

**Mouths Sealed, Limbs Testify**

*“On this Day We will seal their mouths, their hands will speak to Us, and their feet will testify to what they used to commit”* (36:65) describes judgment after being told to burn in Hell (36:63-64). On that Day, al-yawm, Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53), Day when no soul wronged (36:54), mouths sealed, nakhtimu ʿalā afwāhihim, so that they cannot deny, and hands speak, tukallimunā aydīhim, and feet testify, tashhadu arjuluhum, to what they used to commit, bimā kānū yaksibūn. The sealing of mouths prevents lying, denial, argument. The speaking of hands and testimony of feet provide witnesses from own body, ensuring no wrong (36:54) and that reward is based on what they used to do (36:54). The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth and left behind is corroborated by limbs. The town that threatened stoning with hands (36:18) will have hands speak against it; the believing man whose feet rushed from farthest end (36:20) will have feet testify for him.

**Why Mouths Sealed and Hands Speak**

Mouths sealed and hands speak shows that body that was used for disbelief becomes witness against disbeliever. Mouth that mocked messengers (36:30), called them liars (36:15), said *“Why should we feed those whom Allah could have fed?”* (36:47) and *“When will this threat come?”* (36:48) is sealed so it cannot deny. Hands that threatened stoning (36:18), refused to donate (36:47), took other gods hoping to be helped (36:74) will speak about what they did. Feet that walked to reject messengers, to threaten, will testify where they went. The Qur'an says *“On Day when their tongues, hands, and feet will testify against them as to what they used to do”* (24:24) and *“Until when they come to it, their hearing, sight, and skins will testify against them”* (41:20). The testimony of limbs is just because limbs were used for deeds, so they are witnesses. The sūrah says *“Everything is listed in a perfect Record”* (36:12) — record plus limb testimony ensures no wrong.

**The Relation to No Soul Wronged**

This verse ensures *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) because even if mouth denies, limbs testify, so judgment based on true deeds, not denial. The sūrah says *“You will not be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) — what hands and feet did is what they used to do, so testimony of hands and feet is testimony about what they used to do. The town that said *“You are only humans like us, and Most Compassionate revealed nothing, you are simply lying”* (36:15) used mouths to lie; mouths sealed prevents further lying. The believing man who said *“I believe in your Lord, so listen to me”* (36:25) used mouth to declare belief; his mouth would not be sealed in same way, or his limbs would testify for him. The verse thus shows that judgment is embodied, not just based on external record but on body that did deeds.

**How This Verse Connects to Authority**

Sealing mouths and making hands speak and feet testify shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83). The One who created pairs (36:36), night and day, sun and moon (36:37-40), Ark and ships (36:41-42), cattle (36:71-72) also controls mouths, hands, feet, making them speak or sealed by will. The sūrah says *“If We willed, We could have blinded their eyes, so they would struggle to find way. How then could they see?”* (36:66) and *“transfigured them on spot, so they could neither progress nor turn back”* (36:67) — similar control over bodies. Sealing mouths and making hands speak is of same type: God controls bodies. The verse also shows that return to God (36:83) includes return of body parts as witnesses. The body that was used in world is not discarded; it testifies.

**A Verse That Defines True Witness**

Finally, this verse defines true witness as one's own limbs, not just external record. External record can be denied; limb testimony cannot, because limbs were present. The Qur'an says *“This Day We will seal their mouths, their hands will speak to Us, and their feet will testify”* (36:65) — hands speak to Us, to God, indicating direct testimony to Judge, not to humans. Feet testify to what they used to commit, indicating that where feet went is recorded. The verse invites reader to consider what his hands and feet will say on that Day. Will hands say they donated from what Allah provided (36:47) or refused? Will feet say they rushed from farthest end to support messengers (36:20) or walked to threaten stoning (36:18)? The perfect record (36:12) lists what hands and feet did, and on Day they will speak and testify, ensuring no soul wronged and reward based on what was used to do. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of embodied accountability, inviting to use hands and feet now for what will testify for, not against, on Day when mouths sealed.

**Limbs Testifying as Embodied Justice**

Mouths sealed, hands speaking, feet testifying shows embodied justice. Hands that threatened stoning (36:18), refused to donate (36:47), served idols as guards (36:75) will speak against owners; feet that rushed from farthest end to support messengers (36:20) will testify for owners. The sūrah says everything listed in perfect Record (36:12) — record plus limb testimony ensures no soul wronged (36:54). The town that used hands to stone will have hands speak; believing man whose feet rushed will have feet testify. The verse invites to use hands and feet now for what will testify for, not against, on Day when mouths sealed and no bequest or return possible (36:50), by donating, following those who ask no reward (36:21), worshiping Originator (36:22).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:66

> Had We willed, We could have easily blinded their eyes, so they would struggle to find their way. How then could they see

**Expanded Commentary**

**Power to Blind Eyes**

*“Had We willed, We could have easily blinded their eyes, so they would struggle to find their way. How then could they see?”* (36:66) follows sealing mouths and limbs testifying (36:65) with another demonstration of divine power over bodies. Law shaʾnā la-ṭamasnā ʿalā aʿyunihim, had We willed, We could have obliterated their eyes, blinded them, so they would struggle to find way, fa-stabaqū al-ṣirāṭ, they would race to find path, but how could they see, fa-annā yubṣirūn, how would they see if blinded. The verse shows that sight, which they use to turn away from signs (36:46), is gift that could be taken. The sūrah earlier said *“We have placed a barrier before them and behind and covered them up, so they fail to see truth”* (36:9) — failure to see truth is metaphorical blindness; this verse says physical blindness could also be imposed if God willed. The conditional had We willed shows that not blinding is by will and mercy, allowing enjoyment for little while (36:44) and time to see signs — dead earth, gardens, night, sun, moon, Ark, cattle — and to give thanks.

**Why Blinding Eyes Is Mentioned**

Blinding eyes is mentioned because eyes are means to see signs. The sūrah lists many signs that are seen: dead earth revived (36:33), gardens and springs (36:34), pairs (36:36), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases (36:39), sun and moon orbits (36:40), Ark and ships (36:41-42), cattle (36:71-72). All are seen with eyes, yet many turn away (36:46). The verse says God could blind eyes, so they would struggle to find way, showing that ability to see and find way is gift, not entitlement. The Qur'an says *“It is He who made for you hearing, sight, and hearts, little thanks you give”* (23:78). Sight is for thanks, but many do not give thanks (36:35, 36:73). The verse asks how then could they see if blinded, implying they cannot see without God-given sight, so they should use sight to see signs and give thanks, not to turn away.

**The Relation to Barriers and Sealing**

This verse relates to barriers before and behind and covering so they fail to see truth (36:9) and sealing mouths and limbs testifying (36:65). Barriers and covering are metaphorical blindness; blinding eyes would be physical blindness; sealing mouths is preventing speech. All show divine control over senses and limbs. The sūrah says *“And had We willed, We could have transfigured them on spot, so they could neither progress forward nor turn back”* (36:67) next, showing control over movement as well. The three verses together — sealing mouths, blinding eyes, transfiguring — show that God controls speech, sight, movement, and could take any if He willed, but He allows them by mercy for little while (36:44) to give chance to use them rightly. The perfect record (36:12) records how sight was used — to see signs and give thanks or to turn away.

**How Then Could They See as Rhetorical Question**

How then could they see, fa-annā yubṣirūn, is rhetorical question implying they could not see if blinded, so their current seeing is by God's will and mercy. The question also implies that those who have barriers and covering so they fail to see truth (36:9) are like blinded, struggling to find way but unable to see because of metaphorical blindness. The sūrah says *“It is the same whether you warn them or not, they will never believe”* (36:10) for those who have become blinded by persistent turning away. The verse warns that physical blindness could be imposed, but metaphorical blindness already exists for those who turn away, and both prevent finding straight path (36:61). The verse invites to use sight now to find straight path — worship God alone (36:61) — before sight taken or before Day when no soul wronged (36:54) and hands speak and feet testify (36:65).

**A Verse That Defines Sight as Gift**

Finally, this verse defines sight as gift that could be taken, so it should be used for thanks and guidance. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; sight is provision that should lead to thanks. The Qur'an says *“Say, It is He who produced you and made for you hearing, sight, and hearts, little thanks you give”* (67:23). The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of sight as gift, power to take gift, and question how then could they see if taken, inviting to use sight now to see signs — dead earth, gardens, night, sun, moon, Ark, cattle — and to follow those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), worship Originator (36:22), and seek peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) before being among those told burn in Hell today (36:64) with mouths sealed and limbs testifying.

**Sight as Gift That Could Be Taken**

Blinding eyes shows sight is gift that could be taken if God willed, so it should be used to see signs and give thanks. The sūrah lists many signs seen with eyes: dead earth revived (36:33), gardens and springs (36:34), pairs (36:36), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases (36:39), Ark and ships (36:41-42), cattle (36:71-73). All seen, yet many turn away (36:46). The verse says had We willed, We could have blinded eyes, so they would struggle to find way, how could they see — without sight, no way found. The perfect record (36:12) records how sight used. The verse invites to use sight now to find straight path of worshiping God alone (36:61) before sight taken or Day when no soul wronged (36:54).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:67

> And had We willed, We could have transfigured them on the spot, so they could neither progress forward nor turn back

**Expanded Commentary**

**Power to Transfigure on the Spot**

*“And had We willed, We could have transfigured them on the spot, so they could neither progress forward nor turn back”* (36:67) follows power to blind eyes (36:66) with power to transfigure, maskh, transform, freeze. Law shaʾnā la-masakhnāhum ʿalā makānatihim, had We willed, We could have transfigured them in their place, on the spot, so they could neither go forward, fa-mā istaṭāʿū muḍiyyan, nor return, wa lā yarjiʿūn. The image is of people frozen, transformed into something else — stones, animals, or immobilized — in their place, unable to move forward or back. The sūrah earlier described shackles around necks up to chins forcing heads up (36:8) and barriers before and behind and covering so they fail to see (36:9) — immobilization and blindness as metaphorical punishment for turning away. This verse says physical immobilization and transfiguration could also be imposed if God willed, but He allows movement by mercy for little while (36:44). The conditional had We willed shows that not transfiguring is by will and mercy.

**Why Transfiguration on the Spot Is Mentioned**

Transfiguration on spot is mentioned because movement — progress forward and return — is gift that could be taken. The sūrah says *“They will not be able to make a last will, nor can they return to their own people”* (36:50) about those seized by blast while disputing — no return. Transfiguration would be similar: no progress, no return, frozen in place. The verse shows that ability to move, to progress, to return, is gift. The Qur'an says *“It is He who made earth tractable for you, so walk in its tracts and eat of His provision”* (67:15) — walking is by subjection. The sūrah says *“We have subjected these animals to them, so they may ride some and eat others”* (36:72) — riding is by subjection. Movement is gift. The verse asks to use movement to follow straight path (36:61), to rush from farthest end to support messengers (36:20), not to threaten stoning (36:18) or dispute (36:49). The believing man used feet to rush; his feet will testify for him (36:65). Those who use movement to transgress could be transfigured so they cannot move.

**The Relation to Barriers, Blinding, Sealing**

This verse relates to barriers before and behind (36:9) — barriers prevent progress and return; blinding eyes (36:66) prevents seeing way; sealing mouths (36:65) prevents speech; transfiguring on spot prevents movement altogether. All show divine control over senses and limbs. The sūrah emphasizes We for acts only God does — We placed barriers, We sealed mouths, We could blind eyes, We could transfigure. The control is total, but restraint is mercy allowing enjoyment for little while (36:44). The perfect record (36:12) records how movement was used — to rush to truth or to threaten, to donate or refuse, to follow or turn away. On Day when no soul wronged (36:54), hands will speak and feet testify (36:65) about movement. Transfiguration on spot would be immediate immobilization in world; sealing and testimony is immobilization and witness on Day.

**Neither Progress Nor Return as Complete Immobilization**

Neither progress forward nor turn back, fa-mā istaṭāʿū muḍiyyan wa lā yarjiʿūn, indicates complete immobilization, no forward, no backward. Forward, muḍiyy, is going ahead, progressing, advancing; return, rujūʿ, is going back to people, to previous state. The town that was extinguished at once (36:29) could neither progress nor return; those seized by blast while disputing (36:49) could not make bequest nor return (36:50); those transfigured on spot could neither progress nor return. All show that worldly ability to progress and return is temporary and by permission. The sūrah says *“And whoever We grant long life, We reverse them in development. Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) next, showing that long life leads to reversal, inability to progress as before, which is natural transfiguration of aging. The verse invites to use progress and return now for good — progress toward straight path, return to God — before inability.

**A Verse That Defines Movement as Gift**

Finally, this verse defines movement as gift that could be taken, so it should be used for thanks and guidance. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; movement is provision. The Qur'an says *“It is He who made for you earth tractable, so walk in its tracts”* (67:15). Walking is gift. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of movement as gift, power to take gift, and inability to progress or return if taken, inviting to use movement now to follow those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), to worship Originator (36:22), to rush from farthest end to support truth (36:20), before being among those who cannot progress or return, either in world by transfiguration or on Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53) with no soul wronged (36:54) and told either peace (36:58) or burn (36:64).

**Movement as Gift That Could Be Frozen**

Transfiguring on spot so they could neither progress nor turn back shows movement is gift that could be taken. The sūrah says those seized by blast while disputing cannot make bequest nor return to people (36:49-50) — no return. Transfiguration would be similar: frozen, no progress, no return. The town extinguished at once (36:29) could neither progress nor return; those reversed in long life (36:68) lose ability to progress as before. All show movement temporary and by permission. The verse invites to use movement now to rush from farthest end to support truth (36:20), to follow those who ask no reward (36:21), to worship Originator (36:22), before inability, and to prepare for return to God (36:83) where authority over all things.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:68

> And whoever We grant a long life, We reverse them in development. Will they not then understand

**Expanded Commentary**

**Long Life and Reversal in Development**

*“And whoever We grant a long life, We reverse them in development. Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) follows power to blind eyes (36:66) and transfigure on spot (36:67) with natural reversal that happens in long life. Man nuʿammirhu nunakkishu fī al-khalq, whoever We grant long life, We reverse him in creation, development. Nunakkis, reverse, from nakasa, to turn upside down, to invert, indicates that strength becomes weakness, knowledge becomes forgetfulness, upright becomes bent. The Qur'an says *“God creates you, then causes you to die, and among you is he who is brought back to most abject age, so that he knows nothing after having known”* (16:70) and *“Among you is he who is brought back to most abject age”* (22:5). Reversal in development is observable: child grows to strength, then in old age strength declines, senses weaken, memory fades. The verse says We reverse them, attributing reversal to divine act, not just nature, showing that life's stages are by design. The question will they not then understand, a-fa-lā yaʿqilūn, will they not use intellect to see that long life leads to reversal, so worldly life is not permanent and not to be clung to.

**Why Reversal Is a Sign**

Reversal in development is sign because it shows that creation has stages and that this world is not final. The sūrah earlier described dead earth revived (36:33), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases ending like old stalk (36:39) — all show transformation. Long life reversed in development is transformation of human himself, from strength to weakness, which should lead to understanding that just as strength reversed to weakness, weakness can be reversed to strength in resurrection. The sūrah says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81). Creating human first time and reversing in development shows power to recreate again. The verse also shows that long life is not necessarily good; it can be reversal, so quality of life, not length, matters. The believing man who was killed and told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) had short life but honor; those granted long life but not understanding will be reversed and then brought before God (36:32).

**The Relation to Transfiguration and Blinding**

This verse relates to transfiguration on spot (36:67) and blinding eyes (36:66) as natural version of same. Transfiguration on spot would be sudden immobilization; reversal in long life is gradual immobilization through aging. Blinding eyes could be sudden; reversal includes weakening of sight. Sealing mouths (36:65) could be sudden; reversal includes weakening of speech. All show that senses and movement are gifts that can be taken, either suddenly by will or gradually by aging. The sūrah emphasizes We for all — We seal mouths, We could blind eyes, We could transfigure, We reverse in development — showing divine control over body. The restraint from sudden taking is mercy allowing enjoyment for little while (36:44) and time to understand. Reversal in long life is itself sign that should lead to understanding: will they not then understand that life is temporary and return to God (36:83) is coming.

**Will They Not Then Understand as Call to Intellect**

Will they not then understand, a-fa-lā yaʿqilūn, is call to use intellect, ʿaql, to see that long life leads to reversal, so this world not permanent, so preparation for next needed. The sūrah earlier said *“Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense?”* (36:62) about Satan misleading multitudes because sense not used. Here, reversal in development is sign that should make sense obvious: if you see old person reversed, you should understand your own life will reverse if long, so do not cling to world. The Qur'an says *“We have certainly created man in best stature, then We return him to lowest of low, except those who believe and do righteous deeds”* (95:4-6) — best stature then lowest, except believers. Reversal is general; exception is believers who do righteous deeds, whose record remains and whose reward is Paradise busy enjoying (36:55-58). The verse invites to be among exception by believing and doing righteous deeds before reversal.

**A Verse That Defines Life as Stages**

Finally, this verse defines life as stages: grant long life, reverse in development. Life is not static but staged, with growth and reversal, both by divine will. The sūrah says *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead, and write what they send forth and leave behind”* (36:12) — stages of life are recorded, and reversal is part of record. The verse is short, but it holds complete reflection on aging as sign that should lead to understanding that this world is temporary, that return to God is coming, that authority over all things is in His Hands (36:83), and that true success is not long life but forgiveness and honor (36:27) and peace from Merciful Lord (36:58). The reader who sees old person reversed is invited to understand that he too will be reversed if granted long life, and to use current strength to follow Reminder (36:11), follow those who ask no reward (36:21), worship Originator (36:22), give thanks (36:35, 36:73), donate (36:47), before reversal and before being brought before God with no soul wronged (36:54).

**Reversal in Old Age as Reminder of Temporality**

Long life reversed in development shows life staged, with growth then reversal, both by divine will. Child grows to strength, then old age strength declines, senses weaken, memory fades. The Qur'an says among you is he brought back to most abject age so he knows nothing after having known (16:70). Reversal is sign that world not permanent, not to be clung to. The sūrah says dead earth revived (36:33), night stripped of day (36:37), moon phases ending like old stalk (36:39) — all transformations. Long life reversed is transformation of human himself, inviting understanding that just as strength reversed to weakness, weakness can be reversed to strength in resurrection. The verse asks will they not then understand, calling to use intellect to see temporality and prepare for return to God (36:83) while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:69

> We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting for him. This ˹Book˺ is only a Reminder and a clear Quran

**Expanded Commentary**

**Not Poetry, But Reminder and Clear Qur'an**

*“We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting for him. This Book is only a Reminder and a clear Qur'an”* (36:69) defends Prophet against accusation that Qur'an is poetry. The Quraysh said *“He is a poet”* and *“We will wait for him misfortune of time”* (52:30), and *“This is nothing but poetry”*. The verse says We have not taught him poetry, wa mā ʿallamnāhu al-shiʿr, and it is not fitting for him, wa mā yanbaghī lahu, it does not behoove him, it is not appropriate. Poetry, shiʿr, in Arab culture was art of imagination, exaggeration, sometimes falsehood, and poets were seen as inspired by jinn. The Qur'an says *“Poets are followed by the deviators. Do you not see that they wander in every valley and say what they do not do?”* (26:224-226). Teaching poetry would be teaching wandering and saying what not done, which is not fitting for messenger whose duty is clear delivery (36:17) and whose speech is truth. The verse says this Book is only Reminder, dhikr, and clear Qur'an, qurʾān mubīn, not poetry. Reminder, as earlier *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder”* (36:11), is what reminds of origin, return, signs. Clear Qur'an, mubīn, makes clear, as in *“Our duty is only to deliver message clearly”* (36:17).

**Why Poetry Is Denied**

Poetry is denied because Qur'an is not poetry in form or purpose. Form: poetry has meter, rhyme, and is constrained; Qur'an has rhythm but not bound by poetic meter, and its eloquence surpasses poetry. Purpose: poetry often seeks praise, satire, or entertainment; Qur'an seeks guidance, warning, mercy. The sūrah opened with oath *“By the Quran, rich in wisdom”* (36:2) — wisdom, not poetic entertainment. The verse says nor is it fitting for him, indicating that poetry would not suit prophethood. Prophet's character was truthful, trustworthy, not given to poetic exaggeration. The Qur'an says *“He does not speak from desire, it is nothing but revelation revealed”* (53:3-4). Teaching poetry would be teaching from desire and imagination; revelation is from Almighty, Most Merciful (36:5). The denial also protects Qur'an from being dismissed as human art. If it were poetry, it could be matched by poets; since it is not, challenge *“Produce a surah like it”* (2:23) stands, and inability to match shows it is from God.

**This Book Is Only Reminder and Clear Qur'an**

This Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an, in huwa illā dhikrun wa qurʾānun mubīn, defines Qur'an's nature. Only, illā, limits: it is nothing but Reminder and clear Qur'an, not poetry, not magic, not soothsaying. Reminder, dhikr, means it reminds of what is already known in fiṭrah — origin from God, return to God, signs in creation — that was forgotten through heedlessness (36:6). Clear Qur'an, mubīn, means it makes clear, clarifies, as in clear delivery (36:17). The two names together show function: Reminder reminds, clear Qur'an makes clear what is reminded. The sūrah says *“so that you may warn a people whose forefathers were not warned and so are heedless”* (36:6) — Reminder warns heedless. The sūrah says *“to warn whoever is truly alive and fulfil decree against disbelievers”* (36:70) next, showing purpose of Reminder and clear Qur'an: warning living and establishing proof against disbelievers.

**How This Verse Connects to Previous Reversal**

This verse follows reversal in long life (36:68) — will they not then understand — with statement that Qur'an is Reminder and clear Qur'an that should make understand. Reversal in development should lead to understanding that life temporary; Reminder and clear Qur'an make that understanding explicit. The sūrah says *“We have placed in it gardens, caused springs to gush, so they may eat fruit which they had no hand in making. Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:34-35) — will they not give thanks, will they not understand, are similar calls to intellect. The Qur'an as Reminder and clear Qur'an provides content for understanding. The verse also connects to *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder”* (36:11) — Reminder is what is followed; clear Qur'an is what is followed. Those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen (36:11) are those who benefit from Reminder and clear Qur'an.

**A Verse That Defines Qur'an's Genre**

Finally, this verse defines Qur'an's genre: not poetry, but Reminder and clear Qur'an. Genre matters because how a text is classified determines how it is read. If read as poetry, it is read for aesthetic pleasure, not guidance; if read as Reminder and clear Qur'an, it is read for warning and guidance. The verse says We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting, indicating that Prophet's teaching is not poetry but something else — revelation from Almighty, Most Merciful (36:5), rich in wisdom (36:2), upon straight path (36:4). The reader is invited to read Qur'an as Reminder that reminds of origin and return, and as clear Qur'an that makes clear path, not as poetry to be admired then ignored. The sūrah ends *“So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is authority over all things, and to Whom you will all be returned”* (36:83) — authority and return are reminded by Reminder and made clear by clear Qur'an, so that reader may prepare for return while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44).

**Qur'an as Reminder Not Poetry**

We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting, defines Qur'an as Reminder and clear Qur'an, not poetry. Poetry has meter, rhyme, imagination, sometimes falsehood; Qur'an has rhythm but not bound by poetic meter, and its purpose is guidance, warning, mercy. The sūrah opened with oath By the Quran, rich in wisdom (36:2) — wisdom, not entertainment. The verse says this Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an (36:69) — Reminder reminds of origin, return, signs forgotten through heedlessness (36:6); clear Qur'an makes clear path. The sūrah says to warn whoever is truly alive and fulfil word against disbelievers (36:70) — purpose of Reminder and clear Qur'an. The verse invites to read Qur'an as Reminder and clear guide, not as poetry to be admired then ignored, and to follow its straight path (36:61).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:70

> to warn whoever is ˹truly˺ alive and fulfil the decree ˹of torment˺ against the disbelievers

**Expanded Commentary**

**Warning the Living and Establishing Proof**

*“To warn whoever is truly alive and fulfil the decree of torment against the disbelievers”* (36:70) gives purpose of Reminder and clear Qur'an (36:69). Li-yundhira man kāna ḥayyan, to warn whoever is alive, truly alive, and yaḥiqqa al-qawl ʿalā al-kāfirīn, and so that word, decree of torment, may be fulfilled, justified, against disbelievers. The verse divides humanity into two: living who can be warned and disbelievers against whom word fulfilled. Living, ḥayy, here means spiritually alive, whose heart is alive, not dead through heedlessness (36:6), barriers (36:9), turning away (36:46). The Qur'an says *“Is one who was dead and We gave him life and made for him light by which he walks among people like one who is in darkness and cannot emerge?”* (6:122) — alive vs dead. The sūrah earlier said *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen”* (36:11) — those are truly alive. The sūrah said *“It is the same whether you warn them or not, they will never believe”* (36:10) — those are dead, and word fulfilled against them (36:7). The Reminder and clear Qur'an thus has dual purpose: warning living and establishing proof against disbelievers so that word of torment justified.

**Why Truly Alive Matters**

Truly alive, man kāna ḥayyan, indicates that not all who are biologically alive are spiritually alive. The sūrah says *“There is a sign for them in dead earth: We give it life”* (36:33) — dead earth revived is like dead heart revived by Reminder. The Qur'an says *“This Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an, to warn whoever is truly alive”* (36:69-70) — Reminder revives dead hearts as water revives dead earth. The truly alive are those who follow Reminder (36:11), follow those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), worship Originator (36:22), give thanks (36:35, 36:73). Their aliveness is shown by response to warning. The disbelievers are spiritually dead, like dead earth before revival, like those with barriers and covering so they fail to see truth (36:9). For them, word of torment fulfilled, yaḥiqqa al-qawl, same verb as *“The decree has already been justified against most of them”* (36:7) — word becomes true, binding, because they disbelieved. The two purposes — warning living and fulfilling word against disbelievers — are two sides of same Reminder.

**How Word Fulfilled Against Disbelievers**

Word, qawl, decree of torment, becomes fulfilled, justified, against disbelievers because they disbelieved after warning. The sūrah says *“The decree of torment has already been justified against most of them, for they will never believe”* (36:7) — justification comes from disbelief. The Reminder and clear Qur'an establishes proof, so that on Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53) and no soul wronged (36:54), disbelievers cannot say they were not warned. The Qur'an says *“We never punish until We have sent a messenger”* (17:15). Sending Reminder and clear Qur'an is sending messenger with message, so punishment after is just. The word fulfilled is *“I will surely fill Hell with jinn and men all together”* (32:13) and *“Likewise Word of your Lord has come due against those who disbelieve, that they are inhabitants of Fire”* (40:6). The fulfillment is not arbitrary but based on record that lists what they sent forth (36:12) — turning away, refusing to donate, mocking, taking other gods, following Satan.

**The Relation to Previous Verses**

This verse follows *“We have not taught him poetry, nor is it fitting for him. This Book is only Reminder and clear Qur'an”* (36:69) to explain why Reminder and clear Qur'an, not poetry: to warn living and fulfil word against disbelievers. Poetry does not warn living in same way nor establish proof for torment; Reminder and clear Qur'an does. The verse also connects to opening: *“so that you may warn a people whose forefathers were not warned and so are heedless”* (36:6) — warning heedless is purpose; now warning whoever is truly alive is purpose, showing that heedless can become alive if they respond. The sūrah says *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder”* (36:11) — truly alive are those who follow. The verse is thus summary of sūrah's purpose: Qur'an as Reminder and clear Qur'an warns living and establishes proof against disbelievers, so that on Day, busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) and burning in Hell (36:63-64) are both just.

**A Verse That Defines Life and Death**

Finally, this verse defines life and death spiritually: truly alive are those who can be warned by Reminder; dead are disbelievers against whom word fulfilled. The Qur'an says *“You cannot make the dead hear, nor make deaf hear call when they turn away”* (27:80) — dead cannot be warned. The sūrah invites reader to be among truly alive, not among dead against whom word fulfilled, by following Reminder, fearing Most Compassionate unseen (36:11), worshiping Originator (36:22), giving thanks (36:35, 36:73), donating (36:47), and seeking peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44). The verse is short, but it holds complete purpose of revelation: warning living and fulfilling word against disbelievers, so that authority over all things in God's Hands (36:83) is manifest as both mercy that warns and justice that fulfils word, and return to Him (36:83) is to either Paradise or Hell based on whether one was truly alive and warned or dead and word fulfilled.

**Warning Living and Establishing Proof**

To warn whoever is truly alive and fulfil word against disbelievers gives purpose of Reminder and clear Qur'an (36:69). Truly alive are those whose hearts alive, not dead through heedlessness (36:6), barriers (36:9), turning away (36:46). The sūrah says you can only warn those who follow Reminder and fear Most Compassionate unseen (36:11) — those are truly alive. It says it is same whether you warn them or not, they will never believe (36:10) — those are dead, word fulfilled against them (36:7). Reminder and clear Qur'an warns living and establishes proof against disbelievers so that on Day when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53) and no soul wronged (36:54), disbelievers cannot say not warned. The verse invites to be among truly alive, not dead against whom word fulfilled, by following Reminder, worshiping Originator, giving thanks, seeking peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:71

> Do they not see that We singlehandedly created for them, among other things, cattle which are under their control

**Expanded Commentary**

**Cattle Created Singlehandedly and Subjected**

*“Do they not see that We singlehandedly created for them, among other things, cattle which are under their control?”* (36:71) begins final section about cattle and resurrection, after defining Qur'an as Reminder and clear Qur'an to warn living and fulfil word against disbelievers (36:69-70). The verse asks a-lam yaraw, do they not see, have they not considered, that We created for them cattle, anʿām, which are under their control, hum lahā mālikūn, they own them, control them. The phrase singlehandedly, mimmā ʿamilat aydīnā, from what Our Hands have made, indicates creation without partner, by divine Hands, emphasizing power and sole agency. The sūrah earlier said *“We have placed in it gardens and caused springs to gush”* (36:34) and *“We created for them similar things to ride in”* (36:42) — all singlehandedly created. Cattle, anʿām, includes camels, cows, sheep, goats, which were central to Arab life for riding, eating, benefits, drinks. Under their control, mālikūn, means they own, possess, control, indicating subjection. The Qur'an says next that these creatures were made tractable so some serve for transport and others for nourishment (36:72), showing subjection as divine act.

**Why Singlehandedly Matters**

Singlehandedly, from what Our Hands have made, matters because it shows that cattle, like all provision, are from God alone, not from idols or other gods. The disbelievers who have taken other gods besides Allah hoping to be helped (36:74) are reminded that cattle they control were created singlehandedly by God, not by idols. If idols cannot create cattle, they cannot help. The sūrah says *“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75) — idols cannot help, even though served. Cattle that are under control were created by One who can help, so worship should be for Him alone, which is straight path (36:61). The phrase singlehandedly also shows that creation of cattle is easy for God, like creation of heavens and earth (36:81), and like giving fire from green trees (36:80), and like reviving dead earth (36:33). If He can create cattle singlehandedly, He can resurrect decayed bones (36:78-79).

**Do They Not See as Call to Consider**

Do they not see, a-lam yaraw, is call to consider, to look at cattle as sign, similar to *“There is a sign for them in dead earth”* (36:33) and *“There is also a sign for them in night”* (36:37) and *“Another sign for them is that We carried their ancestors in Ark”* (36:41). Cattle are sign for them, for deniers who doubt resurrection and neglect thanks. The sūrah says *“Whenever a sign comes to them from their Lord, they turn away from it”* (36:46) — cattle are sign that should not be turned away from. The verse asks do they not see that We created cattle for them, implying that seeing should lead to thanks and belief. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; cattle are provision that should lead to thanks. The question is rhetorical, expecting yes, they should see, and if they see, they should give thanks and worship Originator (36:22).

**Under Their Control as Subjection**

Under their control, mālikūn, indicates ownership and control, but ownership is by subjection from God, not by inherent power. The next verse says *“These creatures were made subservient so some serve for riding and others for food”* (36:72), showing that control is by subjection, taskhīr. The Qur'an says *“It is He who made cattle for you, that you may ride some and eat some”* (40:79) and *“We made for you cattle, that you may ride and eat”* (23:21). Subjection means cattle obey, allow riding, allow milking, allow slaughter, despite being larger and stronger. That obedience is not from cattle itself but from God who subjected. The verse invites to see control as gift, not entitlement, and to give thanks. The sūrah says *“They derive from them other benefits and drinks. Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:73) — benefits and drinks from cattle should lead to thanks. Control that allows riding and eating is favor that should produce gratitude, not heedlessness (36:6).

**How This Verse Supports Resurrection**

Cattle created singlehandedly and under control supports resurrection argument. If God can create cattle from nothing, singlehandedly, and subject them to humans, He can create humans again from decayed bones. The sūrah later asks *“Do people not see that We have created them from a sperm-drop, then behold, they openly challenge Us?”* (36:77) and *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) — creation from sperm-drop and creation of cattle are similar arguments: creation first time proves ability to create second time. The sūrah says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81). Cattle are part of heavens and earth creation, and Master Creator can create again. The verse is placed after warning living and fulfilling word against disbelievers (36:70) to show that those who disbelieve despite seeing cattle as sign are among those against whom word fulfilled.

**A Verse That Defines Provision as Sign**

Finally, this verse defines provision as sign: cattle created singlehandedly for them, under their control, is sign that should be seen and lead to thanks and belief. The sūrah's catalogue of signs — dead earth, gardens, springs, pairs, night, sun, moon, Ark, ships, cattle — all are provision that are signs. The verse asks do they not see, implying that seeing is expected and that not seeing is heedlessness (36:6) and turning away (36:46). The reader who sees cattle — whether in farm, market, or as meat and milk — is invited to see them as created singlehandedly by God, subjected by God, under control by God's permission, and to give thanks, donate from provision (36:47), worship Originator (36:22), and prepare for return to God (36:83), where authority over all things is in His Hands and where no soul wronged (36:54) and reward based on what was used to do, including thanks for cattle.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:72

> And We have subjected these ˹animals˺ to them, so they may ride some and eat others

**Expanded Commentary**

**Subjection of Cattle for Riding and Eating**

*“And We have subjected these animals to them, so they may ride some and eat others”* (36:72) explains how cattle under their control (36:71) are used. Dhallalnāhā lahum, We have subjected them to them, made them tractable, obedient, subservient, indicates divine act of subjection, taskhīr. The Qur'an says *“We made for you cattle, that you may ride some and eat some”* (40:79, 23:21) and *“It is He who made earth tractable for you”* (67:15) — tractable, subjected, is gift. Subjection means cattle, despite size and strength, allow humans to ride, milk, slaughter, use for benefits. The verse says so they may ride some and eat others, fa-minhā rakūbuhum wa minhā yaʾkulūn, some for riding, some for eating, showing variety of use. Camels and horses for riding, cows, sheep, goats for eating, plus benefits and drinks (36:73). The sūrah says *“Do they not see that We singlehandedly created for them cattle which are under their control?”* (36:71) — singlehandedly created and subjected, so riding and eating are by permission, not entitlement.

**Why Subjection Is Emphasized**

Subjection is emphasized because it shows mercy and power. Mercy, because subjection allows humans to benefit from animals without constant struggle; power, because only God can subject strong animals to weak humans. The Qur'an says *“Glory be to the One who has subjected this to us, and we could not have done it ourselves”* (43:13) about riding. Humans could not subject cattle by themselves; God subjected. The verse says We have subjected, attributing act to God, as with dead earth revived (36:33), gardens placed (36:34), night stripped (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases ordained (36:39), Ark carried (36:41), similar things created to ride (36:42). All are We acts, showing tawḥīd: all provision and subjection from God alone, not idols. The disbelievers who have taken other gods hoping to be helped (36:74) are reminded that subjection that allows riding and eating is from God, not idols, so idols cannot help (36:75).

**Riding Some and Eating Others as Provision**

Riding some and eating others shows variety of provision from same cattle. Some for riding — transport, like Ark and similar things to ride (36:41-42) — some for eating — food, like grain and gardens and springs (36:33-35). The sūrah's catalogue of provision includes both transport and food, both by mercy for little while (36:44). Riding allows movement, seeking provision, trade, pilgrimage; eating allows survival, strength, enjoyment. Both should lead to thanks. The sūrah says *“From them come other uses and drinks, inviting gratitude?”* (36:73) next, adding benefits and drinks to riding and eating, and asking will they not give thanks. The question is repeated after provision, showing that thanks is expected response. The perfect record (36:12) records riding, eating, benefits, drinks, and thanks or ingratitude, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that.

**How Subjection Supports Resurrection**

Subjection of cattle for riding and eating supports resurrection argument. If God can subject strong animals to weak humans, He can subject death to life, making decayed bones live again. The sūrah later says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81). Subjecting cattle is part of creation of heavens and earth, and Master Creator can recreate. The verse also shows that subjection is not permanent in same form; cattle that are ridden and eaten will die, but God can create again. The sūrah says *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead, and write what they send forth and leave behind”* (36:12) — resurrection includes cattle and humans. The One who subjects cattle to humans can subject humans to resurrection, making them rush from graves to Lord (36:51).

**A Verse That Defines Gratitude for Transport and Food**

This verse defines gratitude for transport and food: riding and eating are by subjection from God, so thanks due. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; riding and eating are provision. The Qur'an says *“Eat of what God has provided for you, lawful and good, and give thanks for God's favor, if it is Him you worship”* (16:114). Eating and riding as worship includes saying bismillah, using in obedience, sharing with others. The disbelievers who said *“Why should we feed those whom Allah could have fed?”* (36:47) refused to share provision from cattle and other sources; they will be among those told *“Burn in it today for your disbelief”* (36:64). Those who give thanks by riding to do good, eating with gratitude, donating from provision, will be among those busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, subjection of animals for riding and eating shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. The One who subjects cattle to humans can subject humans to return, making them all be brought before Him (36:32, 36:53). The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of subjection as mercy, provision as sign, and thanks as expected response, inviting reader to see riding and eating as gifts from God, to give thanks, to use riding to follow those who ask no reward and are rightly guided (36:21), to use eating to worship Originator (36:22), and to prepare for return to God, where no soul wronged and reward based on what was used to do, including thanks for subjection.

**Subjection as Mercy Enabling Riding and Eating**

We have subjected these animals to them, so they may ride some and eat others, shows subjection as divine act enabling benefit. The sūrah says We singlehandedly created cattle under their control (36:71) — singlehandedly created and subjected, so riding and eating by permission, not entitlement. Subjection means cattle, despite size and strength, allow riding, milking, slaughter, use for benefits. The verse says some for riding, some for eating, showing variety: camels and horses for riding, cows, sheep, goats for eating, plus benefits and drinks (36:73). The sūrah says will they not then give thanks (36:35, 36:73) for provision; riding and eating should lead to thanks. The perfect record (36:12) records riding, eating, thanks or ingratitude, and on Day no soul wronged (36:54). The verse invites to see riding and eating as gifts from God, to give thanks, and to use riding to follow those who ask no reward and worship Originator, preparing for return to God (36:83).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:73

> And they derive from them other benefits and drinks. Will they not then give thanks

**Expanded Commentary**

**Other Benefits and Drinks From Cattle**

*“And they derive from them other benefits and drinks. Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:73) adds to riding some and eating others (36:72) other benefits, manāfiʿ, and drinks, mashārib. Benefits include wool, hair, leather, bones, manure, labor, etc., from cattle. Drinks include milk, which is pure, palatable, nutritious, coming from between blood and dung, as Qur'an says *“We give you drink from what is in their bellies, between excretion and blood, pure milk, palatable to drinkers”* (16:66). The sūrah earlier mentioned grain for eating (36:33), gardens and springs for fruit (36:34-35), night, sun, moon for timekeeping and light (36:37-40), Ark and ships for carriage (36:41-42), cattle for riding and eating (36:72) — now benefits and drinks complete picture of provision from cattle. All are from God singlehandedly created and subjected (36:71-72), so benefits and drinks are also from God. The question will they not then give thanks, a-fa-lā yashkurūn, is repeated from *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35) after grain and gardens, showing that thanks is expected for all provision, including cattle benefits and drinks.

**Why Benefits and Drinks Are Mentioned Separately**

Benefits and drinks mentioned separately from riding and eating to show variety and abundance. Riding and eating are main uses; benefits and drinks are additional, showing that cattle provide more than just transport and meat. Wool for clothing, leather for shelter, milk for drink, manure for fertilizer, etc., all are benefits that make cattle comprehensive provision. The Qur'an says *“And cattle, He created them for you, in them is warmth and benefits and you eat from them”* (16:5). Warmth, benefits, eating are three categories, similar to riding, eating, benefits and drinks here. The variety shows generosity: one animal provides many things, without human creating animal. The sūrah says *“so that they may eat from its fruit, which they had no hand in making”* (36:35) about gardens; similar applies to cattle: benefits and drinks which they had no hand in making, because animal created singlehandedly by God, not by humans. Thanks due.

**Will They Not Then Give Thanks as Repeated Question**

Will they not then give thanks is repeated question that appears after grain and gardens (36:35) and now after cattle benefits and drinks (36:73), framing provision section with call to thanks. Repetition emphasizes that thanks is central response expected to all provision. Shukr, thanks, in Qur'anic usage is acknowledgment with heart, tongue, limbs, using provision in obedience. The sūrah says *“Whenever a sign comes to them from their Lord, they turn away from it”* (36:46) — turning away is opposite of thanks. Refusing to donate from what Allah provided (36:47) is opposite of thanks. The question will they not then give thanks is thus both invitation and rebuke: provision given, benefits and drinks derived, will they not give thanks? The perfect record (36:12) records thanks or ingratitude, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that. Those who gave thanks will be among those busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with fruits and whatever desired and peace from Merciful Lord; those who did not will be among those told burn in Hell today (36:63-64).

**How Benefits and Drinks Support Resurrection**

Benefits and drinks from cattle support resurrection argument. If God can produce milk, pure drink, from between blood and dung, from animal, He can produce life from decayed bones. The transformation from blood and dung to pure milk is similar to transformation from green tree to fire (36:80) and from dead earth to grain (36:33). All show ability to produce opposite or pure from impure, life from death. The sūrah later says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81). Creating cattle that give benefits and drinks is part of creation of heavens and earth, and Master Creator can recreate. The verse also shows that benefits and drinks are for little while by mercy (36:44), not permanent, so use them for thanks before little while ends with blast seizing while disputing (36:49) and no bequest or return (36:50).

**A Verse That Defines Gratitude as Using Benefits Rightly**

This verse defines gratitude as using benefits and drinks rightly: riding to do good, eating with bismillah and sharing, using wool and leather modestly, drinking milk with thanks, etc. The Qur'an says *“Eat and drink, but be not excessive”* (7:31) — moderation is part of thanks. The disbelievers who refused to donate (36:47) used benefits and drinks without thanks and without sharing. The believing man who rushed from farthest end (36:20) and declared belief (36:25) used his body, time, and life as benefits for truth, and was made among honoured (36:27). His benefits were used rightly, so he is among those having fruits and whatever desired (36:57) and peace (36:58). The verse invites reader to derive benefits and drinks from cattle and other provision, and to give thanks by using them in obedience, donating, worshiping Originator (36:22), following Reminder (36:11), before being brought before God (36:32, 36:53) with no soul wronged.

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, benefits and drinks from cattle show that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. The One who creates cattle singlehandedly, subjects them, allows riding, eating, benefits, drinks, can also take them and resurrect. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of provision as mercy, benefits as test, thanks as expected response, inviting to give thanks now while enjoyment for little while remains, so that when all brought before God, one will be among those busy enjoying, reclining in shade on couches with spouses, having fruits and whatever desired, greeted with peace from Merciful Lord, not among those told step away as wicked and burn in Hell today.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:74

> Still they have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped ˹by them˺

**Expanded Commentary**

**Taking Other Gods Hoping to Be Helped**

*“Still they have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped by them”* (36:74) describes response to provision of cattle and other signs. After being given cattle created singlehandedly, under control, subjected for riding, eating, benefits, drinks, and asked will they not give thanks (36:71-73), they still take other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped. Ittakhadhū min dūnillāh ālihatan laʿallahum yunṣarūn, they took besides God gods, hoping they may be helped. The verb ittakhadhū, took, indicates choice, adoption, not that gods imposed themselves. They took idols, statues, intermediaries, hoping, laʿallahum, perhaps, they may be helped, yunṣarūn, be aided, supported. The sūrah earlier recorded town saying *“You are only humans like us, and Most Compassionate revealed nothing, you are simply lying”* (36:15) and believing man asking *“How could I take besides Him other gods whose intercession would not benefit me nor save me if Most Compassionate intended harm?”* (36:23) and *“Indeed, I would then be clearly astray”* (36:24) if he took other gods. This verse shows that despite all signs and warnings, many still take other gods hoping to be helped.

**Why Hoping to Be Helped Is Futile**

Hoping to be helped by other gods is futile because other gods cannot help, as next verse says *“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve the idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75). The Qur'an says *“They worship besides God what neither harms nor benefits them, and they say These are our intercessors with God”* (10:18) and *“If God touches you with harm, none can remove it but He”* (10:107). Help, naṣr, comes only from God. The sūrah says *“If We willed, We could drown them, then no one would respond to their cries, nor would they be rescued, except by mercy from Us”* (36:43-44) — no responder except mercy from God. Idols cannot respond to cries at sea, cannot rescue from drowning, cannot help in any way. Hoping to be helped by them is misplaced hope. The sūrah says *“How could I take besides Him other gods whose intercession would not benefit me nor save me if Most Compassionate intended harm?”* (36:23) — intercession does not benefit, nor save, so hoping to be helped is vain.

**Still as Indication of Persistence**

Still, wa, indicates persistence despite provision and signs. The sūrah has listed many signs — dead earth revived (36:33), gardens and springs (36:34), pairs (36:36), night, sun, moon (36:37-40), Ark and ships (36:41-42), power to drown and mercy to allow enjoyment (36:43-44), cattle (36:71-73) — yet still they take other gods. The persistence shows heedlessness (36:6), barriers (36:9), turning away whenever sign comes (36:46). The perfect record (36:12) records both provision and taking other gods, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that. The verse is short, but it holds complete diagnosis: provision given, thanks expected, but still other gods taken hoping to be helped, which is clearly astray (36:24) and following Satan (36:60-62) rather than straight path of worshiping God alone (36:61).

**How This Verse Connects to Previous Idolatry Critique**

This verse connects to earlier critique of idolatry: believing man rejected other gods whose intercession does not benefit nor save (36:23) and said he would be clearly astray if he took them (36:24). Now disbelievers do take other gods hoping to be helped, fulfilling exactly what believing man said would be clearly astray. The sūrah says *“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve the idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75) — they serve idols as guards, junud muḥḍarūn, dedicated guards, present, ready, indicating that idol worshipers end up guarding idols, not idols guarding them. The reversal is complete: hoping to be helped by idols, they end up helping idols by guarding them. The Qur'an says *“They worship besides God what neither harms nor benefits”* (10:18) — no benefit, yet they guard. The verse invites to see idolatry as not only futile but inverted: worshipers become servants of what they hoped would serve them.

**A Verse That Defines True Help**

This verse defines true help as from God alone. The Qur'an says *“If God helps you, none can overcome you, but if He forsakes you, who can help you after Him?”* (3:160). Help comes from God, not idols. The sūrah says *“We did not send soldiers from heavens against his people after his death, nor did We need to”* (36:28) — God does not need soldiers to help or punish; idols cannot help at all. The verse invites reader to seek help from God alone, who created cattle singlehandedly (36:71), subjected them (36:72), provides benefits and drinks (36:73), carries in Ark and ships (36:41-42), revives dead earth (36:33), strips night of day (36:37), sets sun fixed term (36:38), ordains moon phases (36:39), and in Whose Hands is authority over all things and to Whom all will be returned (36:83). Seeking help from other gods besides Him is clearly astray (36:24) and leads to being among wicked told to step away (36:59) and burn in Hell (36:63-64), while seeking help from Him alone leads to busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, taking other gods hoping to be helped shows failure to recognize that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. If authority is in His Hands, help must be from Him; if return is to Him, hope should be in Him. Taking other gods hoping to be helped is thus both theological error and practical futility. The verse is short, but it holds complete critique of idolatry as persistent despite provision, as hoping for help from what cannot help, and as leading to serving what one hoped would serve. The reader is invited to abandon taking other gods besides Allah, to worship Him alone, which is straight path (36:61), and to seek help from Him alone, who can help and who greets with peace those who do.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:75

> They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve the idols as dedicated guards

**Expanded Commentary**

**Idols Cannot Help, Though Guarded**

*“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve the idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75) follows *“Still they have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped by them”* (36:74) with statement that idols cannot help, lā yastaṭīʿūna naṣrahum, they are not able to help them, even though they, pagans, are for idols as dedicated guards, wa hum lahum jundun muḥḍarūn, they are for them soldiers present, dedicated guards. The reversal is striking: pagans hope idols will help them, but idols cannot help, and pagans end up serving idols as guards, protecting idols, cleaning them, defending them, rather than idols protecting pagans. The Qur'an says *“They worship besides God what neither harms nor benefits them”* (10:18) and *“If you call upon them, they do not hear your call, and if they heard, they would not respond”* (35:14). Idols cannot help because they have no power, no hearing, no ability. The sūrah earlier said believing man asked *“How could I take besides Him other gods whose intercession would not benefit me nor save me if Most Compassionate intended harm?”* (36:23) — intercession does not benefit, nor save, so they cannot help.

**Why Dedicated Guards Image Matters**

Dedicated guards, jund muḥḍarūn, soldiers present, ready, indicates that idol worshipers become army for idols, guarding them, rather than idols being army for worshipers. The image is of pagans standing guard around idols, protecting them from damage, theft, disrespect, while idols do nothing for them. The Qur'an says *“They serve idols as dedicated guards”* — service is from worshipers to idols, not idols to worshipers. The reversal shows futility: hoping to be helped by idols, they end up helping idols. The sūrah says *“We did not send soldiers from heavens against his people after his death, nor did We need to”* (36:28) — God does not need soldiers; idols need soldiers (guards) because they cannot protect themselves, let alone help others. The verse thus shows that idolatry inverts proper order: Creator should be served, not creation; Helper should be God, not idols that need help.

**The Relation to Previous Verses About Help**

This verse relates to earlier verses about help and rescue: *“If We willed, We could drown them, then no one would respond to their cries, nor would they be rescued, except by mercy from Us”* (36:43-44) — no responder except mercy from God, not idols. The sūrah says *“They have taken other gods besides Allah, hoping to be helped by them”* (36:74) — hoping to be helped, but they cannot help (36:75). The sūrah says *“How could I take besides Him other gods whose intercession would not benefit me nor save me?”* (36:23) — intercession does not benefit, nor save, so they cannot help. The consistent message is that help comes only from God. The Qur'an says *“If God helps you, none can overcome you, but if He forsakes you, who can help you after Him?”* (3:160). Help from God is true help; help from idols is illusion.

**How This Verse Defines Idolatry's Futility**

This verse defines idolatry's futility: idols cannot help, yet worshipers serve them as guards. Futility is double: no help received, service given to what cannot help. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) — serving idols as guards is what they used to do, and reward will be based on that, which is burning in Hell (36:63-64), not help. The perfect record (36:12) records both taking other gods and serving them as guards, and that record will be basis for judgment when all brought before God (36:32, 36:53) with hands speaking and feet testifying (36:65). The verse invites reader to see idolatry as not only theological error but practical waste: serving what cannot help, guarding what cannot guard.

**A Verse That Invites to Seek Help From God Alone**

This verse invites to seek help from God alone, who can help, who carries in Ark and ships (36:41-42), who could drown but allows enjoyment by mercy (36:43-44), who created cattle singlehandedly and subjected them (36:71-72), who provides benefits and drinks (36:73), who revives dead earth (36:33), who strips night of day (36:37), who sets sun fixed term (36:38), ordains moon phases (36:39), and in Whose Hands is authority over all things and to Whom all will be returned (36:83). Seeking help from other gods besides Him is clearly astray (36:24) and leads to being among wicked told to step away (36:59) and burn in Hell (36:63-64), while seeking help from Him alone leads to busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58). The verse is short, but it holds complete critique: idols cannot help, yet worshipers serve them as dedicated guards, showing inversion and futility, and inviting to worship God alone, which is straight path (36:61).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, idols cannot help, though served as guards, shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83), not idols', and return is to Him, not idols. If authority is in His Hands, help must be from Him; if return is to Him, hope should be in Him. Taking other gods hoping to be helped and serving them as guards is thus both error and waste. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of help as from God alone, and invites to abandon idols that cannot help and to serve God alone, who can help and who greets with peace those who do, before being brought before Him with no soul wronged and rewarded only for what was used to do.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:76

> So do not let their words grieve you ˹O Prophet˺. Indeed, We ˹fully˺ know what they conceal and what they reveal

**Expanded Commentary**

**Consolation Not to Grieve Over Words**

*“So do not let their words grieve you O Prophet. Indeed, We fully know what they conceal and what they reveal”* (36:76) is consolation to Prophet after critique of idolaters who cannot help (36:74-75). The sūrah has described disbelievers turning away from signs (36:46), refusing to donate (36:47), mocking threat (36:48), taking other gods hoping to be helped (36:74) that cannot help (36:75). Their words — mocking, calling messengers liars (36:15), bad omens (36:18), saying believers clearly astray (36:47), asking when threat will come (36:48) — grieved Prophet. The verse says fa-lā yaḥzunka qawluhum, so do not let their words grieve you, sadden you. The Qur'an says similar to Prophet elsewhere: *“We know that you are saddened by what they say”* (6:33) and *“So do not let their words grieve you”* (10:65, 36:76). Grieving is natural, but consolation is that God fully knows what they conceal and reveal, innā naʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn, what they hide in hearts and what they show openly. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) — God knows hidden and open, so judgment just, and Prophet need not grieve as if words have power beyond God's knowledge.

**Why Words Grieve and Why Not to Let Them**

Words grieve because they mock, deny, threaten, and Prophet cares for his people. The sūrah says *“Oh pity, such beings! No messenger ever came to them without being mocked”* (36:30) — mocking is painful. The town that threatened stoning (36:18) and killed believing man (36:26) used words to threaten. The Quraysh used words to call Prophet poet, magician, possessed, and to say believers clearly astray (36:47). Such words grieve because they reject truth and harm believers. The verse says do not let their words grieve you, because God knows what they conceal and reveal, so their words are known and will be judged. The perfect record (36:12) lists what they send forth and leave behind, including words, and *“On this Day We will seal their mouths, their hands will speak and feet testify”* (36:65) — mouths that spoke grieving words will be sealed, hands and feet will testify. Knowing that God knows hidden and open should console, because nothing escapes, and justice will be done with no soul wronged (36:54).

**We Fully Know What They Conceal and Reveal**

We fully know what they conceal and reveal, innā naʿlamu mā yusirrūna wa mā yuʿlinūn, emphasizes divine knowledge that encompasses hidden and open. The sūrah says *“That is design of Almighty, All-Knowing”* (36:38) for sun, and *“He has perfect knowledge of every created being”* (36:79) for resurrection, and *“He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) — knowledge is attribute that ensures justice. The verse says We know what they conceal — disbelief, plotting, mocking in hearts — and what they reveal — words that grieve, threats, refusal to donate, mocking threat. Both hidden and open are known, so judgment will be based on complete knowledge, not just open words. The sūrah says *“Everything is listed in a perfect Record”* (36:12) — record includes hidden and open. The verse consoles Prophet that his grieving over words is seen and that God knows words' source in concealed disbelief, so He will judge accordingly.

**How This Verse Connects to Previous Consolation**

This verse connects to earlier consolation: *“You can only warn those who follow Reminder and are in awe of Most Compassionate unseen”* (36:11) — Prophet can only warn those who follow, not all, so not to grieve if some do not follow; *“It is the same whether you warn them or not, they will never believe”* (36:10) — some will never believe, so not to grieve; *“Our duty is only to deliver message clearly”* (36:17) — duty is delivery, not conversion, so not to grieve if not converted. This verse adds that God knows what they conceal and reveal, so Prophet need not carry burden of their words. The sūrah says *“So do not let their words grieve you”* (36:76) — similar to *“We know that you are saddened by what they say, but they do not deny you, rather wrongdoers deny God's signs”* (6:33). Words grieve, but they are denial of God's signs, not just of Prophet, so God knows and will respond.

**A Verse That Defines Prophetic Patience**

This verse defines prophetic patience as not letting grieving words cause despair, because God knows hidden and open. Patience, ṣabr, is required for messenger whose duty is clear delivery (36:17) despite mocking (36:30) and threats (36:18). The believing man who rushed from farthest end (36:20) and declared belief (36:25) and was killed and told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) also faced grieving words and threats, but his patience led to honor (36:27). The Prophet is told to have similar patience, knowing God knows. The verse is short, but it holds complete pastoral theology: words grieve, but do not let them grieve you, because God knows what they conceal and reveal, and He will judge with no soul wronged (36:54), and He will greet with peace those who followed (36:58) and tell wicked to burn (36:63-64). The reader who is grieved by words of mockers today is also consoled: God knows hidden and open, so do not let words grieve, but continue following Reminder, worshiping Originator, giving thanks, donating, seeking peace.

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, We fully know what they conceal and reveal shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him, where knowledge will be manifest. The One who knows hidden and open is One in Whose Hands is authority, so His knowledge ensures just authority. Return to Him is return to One who knows, so no deed, word, concealment, or revelation escapes. The verse invites to live with awareness that God knows hidden and open, to conceal good intention and reveal good deeds, and to not be grieved by words of those who conceal disbelief and reveal mocking, because God knows and will judge, and His greeting of peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) awaits those who persevere.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:77

> Do people not see that We have created them from a sperm-drop, then—behold!—they openly challenge ˹Us˺

**Expanded Commentary**

**Created From Sperm-Drop, Then Openly Challenges**

*“Do people not see that We have created them from a sperm-drop, then behold, they openly challenge Us?”* (36:77) begins final argument for resurrection after consolation not to grieve (36:76). The verse asks a-wa-lam yara al-insān annā khalaqnāhu min nuṭfah, does man not see that We created him from sperm-drop, nuṭfah, tiny fluid, then behold, fa-idhā huwa khaṣīmun mubīn, then he is open challenger, clear disputer, arguing against Us. The sūrah earlier said *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead, and write what they send forth and leave behind”* (36:12) — resurrection asserted; now creation from sperm-drop is evidence that resurrection possible. The Qur'an says *“We created man from sperm-drop, then We made him hearing and seeing”* (76:2) and *“He created man from sperm-drop, yet he is open challenger”* (16:4). Sperm-drop, nuṭfah, is humble origin, tiny, fluid, yet from it comes human who challenges Creator about resurrection, saying *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78). The contrast between humble origin and arrogant challenge is intentional, showing ingratitude and lack of sense.

**Why Sperm-Drop Is Mentioned**

Sperm-drop mentioned because it is origin that everyone knows and that is humble, yet from it comes complex human with hearing, sight, intellect, ability to challenge. If God can create human from tiny fluid, He can recreate from decayed bones, which is similar or easier. The sūrah says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) — creation of heavens and earth is greater than creation from sperm-drop, so resurrection is easy. The verse says We have created them from sperm-drop, attributing creation to God, as with cattle singlehandedly created (36:71) and dead earth revived (36:33). The attribution shows that human existence is not self-produced but from God. The challenge, khaṣīm mubīn, open challenger, clear disputer, indicates that human uses intellect given by God to argue against God, which is ingratitude. The sūrah says *“Will they not then give thanks?”* (36:35, 36:73) for provision; here, will they not see creation from sperm-drop and give thanks rather than challenge.

**Then Behold, They Openly Challenge as Suddenness**

Then behold, fa-idhā, indicates suddenness, surprise: created from humble fluid, then suddenly open challenger. The same fa-idhā appears for town extinguished at once (36:29), blast seizing while disputing (36:49), rushing from graves (36:51), all brought before Us at once (36:53) — sudden transformation. Here, creation from sperm-drop to open challenger is sudden in sense of unexpected ingratitude. The Qur'an says *“Does man not see that We created him from sperm-drop, then behold, he is open challenger”* (16:4, 36:77) — same phrasing, showing that challenging Creator after being created from humble origin is common human failing. The challenge here is about resurrection: *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) — challenging ability to resurrect despite being created from sperm-drop, which is itself creation from near nothing. The verse invites to see that if creation from sperm-drop possible, resurrection from bones possible, so challenge is baseless.

**How This Verse Connects to Previous Consolation**

This verse follows *“So do not let their words grieve you. Indeed, We fully know what they conceal and reveal”* (36:76) — words that grieve include open challenges about resurrection. The Prophet was grieved by those who said *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78), challenging resurrection.
