This verse says do people not see We created them from sperm-drop, then behold, they openly challenge Us — their challenge is known and is ingratitude, so do not grieve, because God knows hidden and open. The perfect record (36:12) records both creation from sperm-drop and open challenge, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that. The verse also connects to *“Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) about reversal in long life — will they not see creation from sperm-drop and understand that resurrection possible.

**A Verse That Defines Ingratitude**

This verse defines ingratitude as being created from humble origin then openly challenging Creator. Ingratitude is not just refusing to give thanks for cattle benefits and drinks (36:73) or for grain and gardens (36:35), but using faculties given — intellect, speech — to challenge Giver. The Qur'an says *“Man is indeed ungrateful to his Lord”* (100:6). Open challenge, khaṣīm mubīn, is clear, evident disputation, not hidden doubt. The sūrah says *“They will cry, Woe to us! Who has raised us from our place of rest? This must be what Most Compassionate warned us of; messengers told truth”* (36:52) — those who challenged openly will cry woe when raised. The verse invites to not be open challenger but to be among those who follow Reminder (36:11), worship Originator (36:22), give thanks (36:35, 36:73), and seek peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, created from sperm-drop then openly challenges shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him. The One who created from sperm-drop has authority to create again, and return is to Him who created first time. The verse is short, but it holds complete argument: humble origin proves ability to recreate, open challenge is ingratitude that will be judged with no soul wronged, and return to Creator who created from sperm-drop is inevitable. The reader who sees his own origin from sperm-drop is invited to give thanks rather than challenge, to believe in resurrection, and to prepare for return to God, where hands will speak and feet testify (36:65) about whether he challenged or thanked. Remembering humble beginning should produce humility now and hope for restoration later, not arrogant dispute.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:78

> And they argue with Us—forgetting they were created—saying, “Who will give life to decayed bones?”

**Expanded Commentary**

**Forgetting Creation, Asking Who Will Give Life to Decayed Bones**

*“And they argue with Us — forgetting they were created — saying, Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) continues argument from sperm-drop (36:77). The disputer, after being created from sperm-drop then openly challenging (36:77), now argues, ḍaraba lanā mathalan wa nasiya khalqahu, presents example, argument, for Us, forgetting his own creation, saying who will give life to bones while they are decayed, ramīm, crumbled, dust. The Qur'an says similar: *“He presents an example for Us and forgets his own creation, saying Who will give life to bones while they are decayed?”* The forgetting is key: he forgets he was created from sperm-drop (36:77), from nothing, from humble origin, yet doubts creation from bones that already existed. The sūrah earlier argued from dead earth revived (36:33), gardens and springs (36:34), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases (36:39), fire from green trees (36:80), creation of heavens and earth (36:81) — all show ability to create from opposite or from nothing, so forgetting own creation is inexcusable. The question who will give life to decayed bones is rhetorical denial, implying no one can, but answer follows: *“They will be revived by the One who produced them first time, for He has perfect knowledge of every created being”* (36:79).

**Why Forgetting Creation Is Mentioned**

Forgetting creation, nasiya khalqahu, is mentioned because it is root of doubt about resurrection. If one remembers he was created from sperm-drop, from dead earth, from nothing, then resurrection from decayed bones is not far-fetched. Forgetting leads to arguing, presenting example for God as if God cannot do. The Qur'an says *“Does man not remember that We created him before, when he was nothing?”* (19:67). Remembering creation before when nothing should make resurrection from something — bones — seem easy. The sūrah says *“Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) about reversal in long life — will they not remember creation and understand resurrection. The disputer forgets, so he argues. The perfect record (36:12) records both forgetting and arguing, and *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) will be based on that. The verse shows that doubt about resurrection stems not from lack of evidence but from forgetting evidence already given — own creation.

**Who Will Give Life to Decayed Bones as Mocking Question**

Who will give life to decayed bones, man yuḥyī al-ʿiẓām wa hiya ramīm, is mocking question, implying impossibility. The disputer holds decayed bones, crumbles them, says who can give life to these. The Qur'an records similar: *“When we are bones and crumbled dust, will we be raised as new creation?”* (17:49, 17:98, 23:35, 37:16). The question assumes that decayed, crumbled bones cannot be revived because they are dust. The sūrah answers with *“They will be revived by the One who produced them first time”* (36:79) and *“He is the One who gives you fire from green trees, and behold, you kindle fire from them”* (36:80) — fire from green, wet tree is more surprising than life from dry bones, yet it happens, so life from bones possible. The sūrah also says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) — creation of heavens and earth greater than bones, so resurrection easy.

**How This Verse Connects to Sperm-Drop Argument**

This verse connects to sperm-drop argument (36:77): created from sperm-drop, then openly challenges, and argues forgetting creation, saying who will give life to decayed bones. The sequence is: creation from humble origin, challenge, forgetting, arguing about bones. The sequence shows ingratitude and lack of sense. The sūrah says *“Yet he already misled great multitudes of you. Did you not have any sense?”* (36:62) — not using sense leads to forgetting creation and arguing. The believing man who said *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me, and to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) remembered creation and return, so he did not argue about bones but believed. His reward was *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) and being among honoured (36:27). The disputer who forgets creation and argues about bones will be among those told *“Burn in it today for your disbelief”* (36:63-64).

**A Verse That Defines Doubt as Forgetting**

This verse defines doubt about resurrection as forgetting own creation. Doubt is not intellectual but moral — forgetting. The Qur'an says *“Does man not remember that We created him before, when he was nothing?”* (19:67) — remembering would remove doubt. The sūrah invites to remember creation from sperm-drop, from dead earth, from pairs, from night and day, etc., and to understand that One who created first time can create second time. The verse is short, but it holds complete diagnosis: arguing with God, forgetting own creation, asking who will give life to decayed bones, which is answered next that One who produced first time will revive, for He has perfect knowledge of every created being (36:79). The reader who remembers his own creation from humble origin is invited to not argue but to believe, to give thanks (36:35, 36:73), to follow Reminder (36:11), and to prepare for return to God (36:83) where no soul wronged.

**The Link to Knowledge and Authority**

Finally, forgetting creation and asking who will give life to decayed bones shows failure to recognize that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and that He has perfect knowledge of every created being (36:79). The One who created from sperm-drop has knowledge of every bone, every crumb, every decayed particle, so He can revive. The verse is followed by answer that emphasizes knowledge: He has perfect knowledge of every created being (36:79). The disputer forgets creation, but God does not forget; His knowledge encompasses decayed bones. The verse invites to trust that knowledge and authority, to not argue with God forgetting own creation, but to remember creation and believe that He who produced first time will revive, and to seek peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) rather than burning in Hell for disbelief (36:64).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:79

> Say, ˹O Prophet,˺ “They will be revived by the One Who produced them the first time, for He has ˹perfect˺ knowledge of every created being

**Expanded Commentary**

**Answer: Revived by One Who Produced First Time**

*“Say, O Prophet, They will be revived by the One who produced them the first time, for He has perfect knowledge of every created being”* (36:79) answers the disputer's question *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78). The command qul, say, O Prophet, indicates that answer is from God through messenger, not Prophet's own reasoning. The answer has two parts: they will be revived by the One who produced them first time, yuḥyīhā alladhī anshaʾahā awwala marrah, and He has perfect knowledge of every created being, wa huwa bi-kulli khalqin ʿalīm. First part argues from first creation: producing first time proves ability to produce second time, and second is easier than first. The Qur'an says *“It is He who originates creation, then repeats it, and that is easier for Him”* (30:27) and *“Does man not remember that We created him before, when he was nothing?”* (19:67). If creation from nothing possible, creation from something — decayed bones — is easier. Second part argues from knowledge: He has perfect knowledge of every created being, so He knows every bone, every particle, every decayed crumb, and can reassemble.

**Why First Creation Proves Second**

First creation proves second because first is from nothing or from humble sperm-drop (36:77), while second is from existing bones, even if decayed. The One who produced first time, anshaʾahā awwala marrah, originated, brought into existence first time, has power to bring again. The sūrah earlier argued from dead earth revived (36:33), gardens and springs (36:34), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases (36:39), fire from green trees (36:80), creation of heavens and earth (36:81) — all show ability to create and transform, so reviving bones is consistent. The disputer forgot his own creation (36:78), so answer reminds: One who produced first time will revive. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth includes first creation and will include second. The sūrah says *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead”* (36:12) — assertion now explained: We resurrect because We produced first time and have perfect knowledge.

**Perfect Knowledge of Every Created Being**

He has perfect knowledge of every created being, bi-kulli khalqin ʿalīm, means He knows every creature, every bone, every particle, every stage. The sūrah says *“That is design of Almighty, All-Knowing”* (36:38) for sun, and *“He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) — knowledge ensures ability to recreate accurately. If God knows every created being perfectly, He can reconstruct decayed bones perfectly, with no error, no confusion. The Qur'an says *“Does He who created not know? And He is Most Subtle, All-Aware”* (67:14). Knowledge of creation implies ability to recreate. The disputer's doubt — who will give life to decayed bones — assumes that decayed bones are lost, unknown, unrecoverable. Answer says He has perfect knowledge of every created being, so nothing lost, nothing unknown, so revival possible and precise. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) — perfect knowledge ensures no wrong, because every deed and every bone known.

**How This Verse Answers Forgetting**

This verse answers forgetting creation (36:78) by reminding of first creation and perfect knowledge. The disputer forgot his own creation, but God did not forget; He produced first time and knows every created being. Forgetting is human failing; perfect knowledge is divine attribute that compensates. The sūrah says *“Will they not then understand?”* (36:68) about reversal in long life — understanding requires remembering creation. The answer to who will give life to decayed bones is: One who produced first time and has perfect knowledge will. The verse invites reader to remember first creation — from sperm-drop (36:77), from dead earth (36:33), from pairs (36:36) — and to trust perfect knowledge that encompasses all. The believing man who remembered origination and return (36:22) did not forget, so he believed and was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26).

**A Verse That Defines Resurrection as Easy**

This verse defines resurrection as easy because first creation proves ability and perfect knowledge ensures accuracy. The Qur'an says *“It is He who originates, then repeats, and that is easier for Him”* (30:27). Second creation easier than first, so doubt baseless. The sūrah later says *“All it takes, when He wills something to be, is simply to say to it: Be! And it is”* (36:82) — Be revives bones as easily as Be created first time. The verse is short, but it holds complete answer to doubt: revived by One who produced first time, for He has perfect knowledge of every created being. The reader who doubts resurrection is invited to remember first creation and perfect knowledge, to give thanks for creation (36:35, 36:73), to follow Reminder (36:11), and to prepare for return to God (36:83) where no soul wronged and reward based on what was used to do.

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, revived by One who produced first time for He has perfect knowledge shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) and return is to Him who produced first time. The One who produced first time has authority to produce second time, and return is to Him. The verse is placed before fire from green trees (36:80) and creation of heavens and earth (36:81) and Be and it is (36:82) and glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority and to Whom all will be returned (36:83), forming climax of resurrection argument: first creation, perfect knowledge, fire from green trees, heavens and earth, Be, authority and return. The progression shows that resurrection is not isolated miracle but part of consistent divine power and knowledge that runs through sūrah. The reader is invited to say, as commanded, they will be revived by One who produced first time, for He has perfect knowledge, and to live accordingly.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:80

> ˹He is the One˺ Who gives you fire from green trees, and—behold!—you kindle ˹fire˺ from them

**Expanded Commentary**

**Fire From Green Trees**

*“He is the One who gives you fire from green trees, and behold, you kindle fire from them”* (36:80) gives another proof of ability to produce opposite from same, supporting resurrection. Green trees, shajar akhḍar, are wet, alive, full of water, yet fire, nār, comes from them, and you kindle fire from them, fa-idhā antum minhu tūqidūn, then behold, you kindle from them. The transformation is surprising: green, wet tree producing fire, which is hot, dry, opposite. Arabs knew two trees, markh and ʿafār, that when rubbed together produce fire even when green, or they knew that fire can be kindled from green wood by friction. The verse says He is the One who gives you fire from green trees, attributing transformation to God, not to nature alone. The sūrah earlier said dead earth revived with grain (36:33), night stripped of day (36:37), sun fixed term (36:38), moon phases like old stalk (36:39), cattle benefits and drinks from between blood and dung (36:73) — all transformations. Fire from green trees is similar: opposite from same, showing power to bring life from death and death from life.

**Why Fire From Green Trees Supports Resurrection**

Fire from green trees supports resurrection because it shows ability to produce one thing from its opposite. Green tree is wet, alive; fire is dry, consuming. If God can produce fire from green tree, He can produce life from decayed bones, which is also opposite — life from dead. The Qur'an says *“He brings living from dead and dead from living”* (3:27, 30:19). Fire from green trees is example of living (tree) producing something that consumes (fire), or wet producing dry, showing that opposites can come from same source by divine will. The sūrah says *“They will be revived by the One who produced them first time, for He has perfect knowledge”* (36:79) — producing first time and producing fire from green trees both show power to produce. The verse says behold, you kindle fire from them, fa-idhā, then at once, you kindle, showing immediacy and ease: green trees, then behold, fire kindled. Similarly, decayed bones, then behold, revived. The ease is same.

**The Relation to Previous Arguments**

This verse follows answer that they will be revived by One who produced first time and has perfect knowledge (36:79) with additional proof: fire from green trees. The sequence is: creation from sperm-drop (36:77), forgetting creation and asking who will give life to decayed bones (36:78), answer that One who produced first time will revive for He has perfect knowledge (36:79), then fire from green trees as additional proof (36:80), then creation of heavens and earth (36:81), then Be and it is (36:82), then glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority and to Whom all will be returned (36:83). The progression moves from human creation to fire from trees to cosmic creation to divine command to authority and return, each step showing greater power, making resurrection increasingly reasonable. Fire from green trees is middle step, showing transformation in familiar, daily experience — kindling fire — that points to greater transformation — reviving bones.

**How This Verse Was Understood**

Early commentators explained fire from green trees as referring to specific trees that produce fire when rubbed, or as general principle that fire comes from wood that was once green, or that green tree contains fire potentially. The Qur'an says *“Have you seen the fire you kindle? Is it you who produce its tree, or are We the Producer?”* (56:71-72) — similar question about fire's origin. The answer is We are Producer. The verse says He is the One who gives you fire from green trees, attributing fire's origin to God, not to trees themselves. The sūrah says *“so that they may eat from its fruit, which they had no hand in making”* (36:35) — fruit not made by hands; fire from green trees not made by hands either, but given by God. The verse invites to see fire kindled from green trees as gift and sign, not just tool, and to give thanks, as with other provisions.

**A Verse That Defines Transformation**

This verse defines transformation as divine act: green trees to fire, dead earth to grain, night to day, sperm-drop to challenger, decayed bones to living. All are transformations by divine will and knowledge. The sūrah says *“All it takes, when He wills something to be, is simply to say to it: Be! And it is”* (36:82) — Be transforms. Fire from green trees is example of Be — green tree, Be fire, and it is, you kindle. The verse is short, but it holds complete proof: if green, wet tree can give fire, dry, hot, then decayed, dry bones can give life, moist, alive, because both are opposite transformations by same power. The reader who kindles fire from wood is invited to see in that act a sign of resurrection, to remember that One who gives fire from green trees can give life to decayed bones, and to prepare for return to God (36:83) where no soul wronged (36:54).

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, fire from green trees shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83), including authority to produce fire from green and life from dead. The One who gives fire from green trees has authority over opposites, so He has authority over death and life. Return to Him is return to One who can produce fire from green and life from bones. The verse is placed before creation of heavens and earth (36:81) and Be and it is (36:82), showing that transformation in small — fire from tree — points to transformation in large — heavens and earth, resurrection. The reader is invited to see daily kindling as reminder of final kindling — resurrection — and to live with awareness that He who gives fire from green trees will give life to decayed bones, and to Whom all will be returned.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:81

> Can the One Who created the heavens and the earth not ˹easily˺ resurrect these ˹deniers˺?” Yes ˹He can˺! For He is the Master Creator, All-Knowing

**Expanded Commentary**

**Creator of Heavens and Earth Can Resurrect**

*“Can the One who created the heavens and the earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is the Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) is climax of resurrection argument. After creation from sperm-drop (36:77), forgetting and arguing about decayed bones (36:78), answer that One who produced first time will revive for He has perfect knowledge (36:79), fire from green trees (36:80), now creation of heavens and earth is cited as greater than resurrection, so if greater possible, lesser possible. A-wa-laysa alladhī khalaqa al-samāwāti wa al-arḍ bi-qādirin ʿalā an yakhluqa mithlahum, is not the One who created heavens and earth able to create like them, i.e., resurrect deniers. The answer is balā, yes, indeed, He can, wa huwa al-Khallāq al-ʿAlīm, for He is Master Creator, All-Knowing. Khallāq, Master Creator, intensive form, indicating One who creates repeatedly, abundantly, with mastery. ʿAlīm, All-Knowing, indicates knowledge of every creation. The verse argues a fortiori: if heavens and earth — vast, complex — created, then resurrecting humans — small — is easy.

**Why Heavens and Earth Are Mentioned**

Heavens and earth mentioned because they are greatest creation visible, and their creation is greater than human creation. The Qur'an says *“Creation of heavens and earth is greater than creation of mankind, but most of mankind do not know”* (40:57). If God can create heavens and earth, which are greater, He can create humans again, which is smaller. The sūrah earlier listed signs in heavens and earth — dead earth, gardens, night, sun, moon, Ark, cattle — all part of heavens and earth creation. Now it says Creator of all those can resurrect. The verse says can He not easily resurrect these deniers, indicating that deniers are like them, mithlahum, similar to them, i.e., humans like those created first time. The ability to create heavens and earth includes ability to create like humans again. The answer yes, He can, for He is Master Creator, All-Knowing, emphasizes that creation is not one-time but ongoing, mastery, and knowledge ensures accurate recreation.

**Master Creator, All-Knowing as Names**

Master Creator, Khallāq, All-Knowing, ʿAlīm, are names that combine power and knowledge, like Almighty, All-Knowing (36:38) for sun. Khallāq indicates One who creates repeatedly, not just once, so resurrection is repetition of creation, which is characteristic of Master Creator. ʿAlīm indicates knowledge of every created being (36:79), so recreation will be accurate. The sūrah says *“They will be revived by the One who produced them first time, for He has perfect knowledge of every created being”* (36:79) — perfect knowledge now repeated as All-Knowing, showing consistency. The verse is thus summary of previous: first creation, perfect knowledge, fire from green trees, heavens and earth — all point to Master Creator, All-Knowing who can resurrect. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth is possible because All-Knowing knows.

**How This Verse Answers Doubt**

This verse answers doubt *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) with a fortiori: if heavens and earth created, then bones can be revived. The disputer forgot his own creation (36:78), but heavens and earth are visible creation that he cannot deny, so argument from greater to smaller is compelling. The Qur'an says *“Do they not see that God who created heavens and earth and was not wearied by their creation is able to give life to dead? Yes, indeed, He is able to do all things”* (46:33). Not wearied indicates ease. The sūrah says *“When He wills a thine wills something to be, is simply to say to it: Be! And it is”* (36:82) next, showing ease. The verse invites to look at heavens and earth — sun traveling fixed term (36:38), moon phases (36:39), night stripped of day (36:37), dead earth revived (36:33) — and to conclude that Creator of all that can easily resurrect deniers.

**A Verse That Defines Ease of Resurrection**

This verse defines ease of resurrection: if heavens and earth created, then resurrecting deniers easy, because Master Creator, All-Knowing can do. The verse says can He not easily resurrect, indicating that resurrection is easy, not difficult, for such Creator. The sūrah says *“It is certainly We who resurrect the dead”* (36:12) as assertion; now ease explained. The verse is short, but it holds complete a fortiori argument that is central to Qur'anic resurrection discourse. The reader who sees heavens and earth daily is invited to see them as proof that resurrection is easy, to not be among those who argue forgetting creation, but among those who follow Reminder (36:11) and are busy enjoying in Paradise (36:55-58) with peace from Merciful Lord (36:58).

**The Link to Be and Authority**

Finally, Creator of heavens and earth who is Master Creator, All-Knowing can resurrect easily, and next verse says all it takes is Be and it is (36:82), and final verse says glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority over all things and to Whom all will be returned (36:83). The three verses together — heavens and earth Creator can resurrect, Be and it is, authority over all things and return — form climax: power to create heavens and earth, ease of Be, authority and return. The progression shows that resurrection is not only possible but easy and under authority of One to Whom return is. The reader is invited to trust Master Creator, All-Knowing, who created heavens and earth, who can easily resurrect deniers, and to prepare for return to Him by worshiping Him alone, which is straight path (36:61), before being brought before Him with no soul wronged (36:54).
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:82

> All it takes, when He wills something ˹to be˺, is simply to say to it: “Be!” And it is

**Expanded Commentary**

**Be and It Is**

*“All it takes, when He wills something to be, is simply to say to it: Be! And it is”* (36:82) describes divine command that makes resurrection and all creation easy. Innama amruhu idhā arāda shayʾan an yaqūla lahu kun fa-yakūn, His command when He wills something is only to say to it Be and it is. The verse emphasizes ease and immediacy: will, say Be, and it is. No process, no delay, no difficulty. The sūrah earlier said single blast extinguished town at once (36:29), single blast seizes while disputing (36:49), single blast brings all before God at once (36:53) — all are examples of Be and it is. The verse says all it takes, innamā, only, indicating exclusivity: only saying Be is needed. The Qur'an says similar in *“When We will something, Our only word is Be and it is”* (16:40) and *“His command is only Be and it is”* (2:117, 3:47, 19:35, 40:68). The command Be is kun, imperative, and its result fa-yakūn, and it is, shows immediate fulfillment.

**Why Be and It Is Supports Resurrection**

Be and it is supports resurrection because it shows that resurrection does not require assembling bones gradually, but only divine command Be. The disputer asked *“Who will give life to decayed bones?”* (36:78) thinking revival requires difficult reassembly; answer is One who produced first time and has perfect knowledge (36:79) and gives fire from green trees (36:80) and created heavens and earth (36:81) can simply say Be and bones revived. The sūrah says *“Can the One who created heavens and earth not easily resurrect these deniers? Yes, He can! For He is Master Creator, All-Knowing”* (36:81) — Master Creator's way is Be. The verse thus explains how resurrection happens: by command Be. The perfect record (36:12) that lists what they sent forth is not needed for God to know how to reconstruct; Be reconstructs. The record is for justice and for humans to see, but divine power needs only Be.

**The Relation to Previous Blasts**

Be and it is relates to blasts: single blast that extinguished town (36:29) and seizes while disputing (36:49) and brings all before God (36:53) are all instances of Be. Blast is sound that is command, like Be, that produces effect instantly. The sūrah says *“All it took was one mighty blast, and they were extinguished at once”* (36:29) — blast as Be that extinguishes. *“It will only take one Blast, then at once they will all be brought before Us”* (36:53) — blast as Be that gathers. Be and it is is the principle behind blasts: divine command produces immediate result. The verse also relates to *“We did not send soldiers from heavens against his people after his death, nor did We need to”* (36:28) — no soldiers needed because Be suffices. The town that threatened painful punishment from itself (36:18) needed many people and stones; God needs only Be.

**How Be Shows Ease**

Be shows ease: all it takes is saying Be. The Qur'an says *“Creation of you all and resurrection of you all is but as a single soul”* (31:28) — easy as one. The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged”* (36:54) — ease of Be does not lead to injustice; perfect knowledge ensures justice. The verse invites to see that what seems difficult for humans — reviving decayed bones, creating heavens and earth, giving fire from green trees — is easy for God, requiring only Be. The believing man who was told *“Enter Paradise”* (36:26) entered by Be — command Enter Paradise, and he entered. The wicked told *“Burn in it today for your disbelief”* (36:64) burn by Be — command burn, and they burn. Both outcomes are by Be, showing authority.

**A Verse That Defines Divine Command**

This verse defines divine command as Be and it is, showing that God's will and speech are effective, not mere wish. The sūrah says *“So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is authority over all things, and to Whom you will all be returned”* (36:83) next, indicating that authority over all things includes authority to say Be and it is, and return is to One who says Be. The verse is short, but it holds complete theology of divine command: will, say Be, and it is, immediate, easy, effective. The reader who hears Be now as reminder is invited to respond before forced Be that brings all before God. The Qur'an says *“The matter of Hour is but as blinking of eye, or nearer”* (16:77) — the divine "Be, and it is" is as swift as blinking. The verse invites to prepare now, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following Reminder (36:11), worshiping Originator (36:22), giving thanks (36:35, 36:73), seeking peace from Merciful Lord (36:58), before Be brings all before Him.

**The Link to Authority and Return**

Finally, Be and it is shows that authority over all things is in God's Hands (36:83) because authority is exercised by saying Be and it is. Return to Him is return to One who says Be, so return is inevitable and easy for Him. The verse is climax of sūrah's argument for resurrection: from dead earth to gardens to night to sun to moon to Ark to cattle to sperm-drop to bones to first creation to perfect knowledge to fire from green trees to heavens and earth to Be to authority and return. Each step shows greater power, culminating in Be and authority and return. The reader is invited to glorify One who says Be and it is, and to Whom all will be returned, and to live now in way that leads to hearing peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) rather than burn in Hell today (36:64) when Be brings all before Him.
## Sūrah Yā-Sīn 36:83

> So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is the authority over all things, and to Whom ˹alone˺ you will ˹all˺ be returned

**Expanded Commentary**

**Glory to the One in Whose Hands Is Authority and to Whom Return Is**

*“So glory be to the One in Whose Hands is the authority over all things, and to Whom alone you will all be returned”* (36:83) concludes the sūrah with tasbīḥ, glorification, like *“Glory be to the One who created all things in pairs”* (36:36) earlier, forming inclusio. Fa-subḥāna alladhī bi-yadihi malakūtu kulli shayʾin wa ilayhi turjaʿūn, so glory be to the One in Whose Hands is malakūt, authority, dominion, sovereignty, of all things, and to Him you will all be returned. The verse has two clauses: authority over all things in His Hands, and return to Him. Authority, malakūt, intensive form of mulk, kingdom, indicating complete, all-encompassing dominion, not just partial. The sūrah has shown authority throughout: We placed barriers (36:9), We resurrect dead and write what they send forth (36:12), We carried ancestors in Ark (36:41), We created similar things to ride (36:42), We could drown them (36:43), We could blind eyes (36:66), We could transfigure on spot (36:67), We reverse in development (36:68), We created from sperm-drop (36:77), We give fire from green trees (36:80), We say Be and it is (36:82). All are authority. Return, turjaʿūn, you will all be returned, is final destination, as earlier *“Why should I not worship the One who originated me, and to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) and *“They will all be brought before Us”* (36:32, 36:53).

**Why Glory Be Here**

Glory be, subḥāna, is said when seeing something amazing that indicates God's transcendence and perfection. After listing signs — dead earth, gardens, pairs, night, sun, moon, Ark, ships, cattle, sperm-drop, bones, fire from green trees, heavens and earth, Be — and scenes — town extinguished, believing man honored, Paradise busy enjoying with peace, Hell burning — the appropriate response is glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority over all things. The sūrah began with *“Yā Sīn. By the Quran, rich in wisdom!”* (36:1-2) — wisdom — and ends with glory be to One with authority and return — transcendence and accountability. The progression is from wisdom to authority and return, showing that wise Qur'an calls to recognize authority and prepare for return. The verse says so, fa, indicating conclusion: because of all that preceded, so glory be. The conclusion is logical: if all signs and scenes true, then glory be to One with authority and to Whom return.

**Authority Over All Things in His Hands**

Authority over all things in His Hands, bi-yadihi malakūtu kulli shayʾin, indicates that all dominion, all control, all ownership is in His Hands, not in idols that cannot help (36:74-75), not in town that threatened punishment from itself (36:18), not in Satan who misled multitudes (36:62). The sūrah says *“They cannot help the pagans, even though they serve idols as dedicated guards”* (36:75) — idols have no authority; *“If We willed, We could drown them, then no one would respond to their cries, nor would they be rescued, except by mercy from Us”* (36:43-44) — no responder except Him, showing authority. The phrase in His Hands, bi-yadihi, indicates possession and control, as in *“In His Hands is authority”* (67:1). Authority over all things includes authority to revive dead earth, to strip night of day, to set sun fixed term, to ordain moon phases, to carry in Ark, to subject cattle, to create from sperm-drop, to revive decayed bones, to give fire from green trees, to create heavens and earth, to say Be and it is. All are authority.

**And to Whom You Will All Be Returned**

And to Whom you will all be returned, wa ilayhi turjaʿūn, is final statement of accountability. Return to Him is inevitable, as earlier *“to Whom you will be returned”* (36:22) said by believing man, and *“they will all be brought before Us”* (36:32, 36:53). Return is to One with authority, so return is to Judge who has authority to reward and punish with no soul wronged (36:54). The sūrah says *“On that Day no soul will be wronged, nor will you be rewarded except for what you used to do”* (36:54) — return is for recompense based on what was sent forth and left behind recorded in perfect Record (36:12) with hands speaking and feet testifying (36:65). The verse says you will all be returned, turjaʿūn, you, all, including believers, disbelievers, town, man, ancestors, those in ships, cattle, all. No one escapes return. The believing man returned to Paradise (36:26); his killers returned to Hell (36:63-64). Return is universal, outcome differs.

**How This Verse Concludes the Sūrah**

This verse concludes sūrah by summarizing its two main themes: authority and return. Authority over all things explains how signs happen — dead earth revived, night stripped, sun fixed, moon phases, Ark carried, cattle subjected, sperm-drop created, bones revived, fire from green trees, heavens and earth created, Be and it is. Return to Him explains why signs matter — to prepare for return, to give thanks, to follow Reminder, to worship Originator, to seek peace. The sūrah began with *“Yā Sīn”* (36:1) — mysterious letters whose meaning with God — and ends with glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority and to Whom return — mystery and accountability. The inclusio with *“Glory be to the One who created all things in pairs”* (36:36) shows that sūrah begins and ends with glorification, framing all signs and scenes with tasbīḥ. The final verse invites reader to glorify One with authority and to prepare for return, while enjoyment for little while remains (36:44), by following straight path of worshiping God alone (36:61), which leads to peace from Merciful Lord (36:58) rather than burning in Hell (36:64).

**A Verse That Defines Proper Response**

Finally, this verse defines proper response to sūrah as glorification and preparation for return. So glory be, fa-subḥāna, is response of awe, declaring God free from imperfection, partner, inability. The One in Whose Hands is authority over all things deserves glory; return to Him deserves preparation. The sūrah says *“So do not let their words grieve you. Indeed, We fully know what they conceal and reveal”* (36:76) — grieving words are from those who do not glorify nor prepare for return. The verse is short, but it holds complete conclusion: authority over all things in His Hands, return to Him, so glory be to Him. The reader who has traversed sūrah from Yā Sīn through oath by wise Qur'an, messenger upon straight path, revelation from Almighty, Most Merciful, warning heedless, decree justified, shackles, barriers, two audiences, resurrection and record, town example, believing man, single blast, pity for mockers, past destructions, universal arraignment, dead earth, gardens, pairs, night, sun, moon, Ark, ships, drowning and mercy, call to be mindful, turning away, refusal to donate, mocking threat, blasts, no bequest, trumpet, woe, single blast bringing all, no soul wronged, Paradise busy enjoying with shade, couches, fruits, desires, peace, separation of wicked, command not to follow Satan but worship God alone as straight path, Satan misleading multitudes, Hell warned of and burning, sealing mouths and limbs testifying, power to blind and transfigure, reversal in long life, not poetry but Reminder and clear Qur'an to warn living and fulfil word, cattle created and subjected with benefits and drinks, taking other gods hoping to be helped but cannot help though served as guards, not letting words grieve because God knows hidden and open, creation from sperm-drop then challenging, arguing forgetting creation about decayed bones, answer that One who produced first time will revive for He has perfect knowledge, fire from green trees, Creator of heavens and earth can resurrect as Master Creator, All-Knowing, Be and it is — after all that, proper response is glory be to One in Whose Hands is authority over all things and to Whom all will be returned.
